### Towards Understanding Bias in Synthetic Data for Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10301v1)
- **Authors**: Hossein A. Rahmani, Varsha Ramineni, Nick Craswell, Bhaskar Mitra, Emine Yilmaz
- **Abstract**: Test collections are crucial for evaluating Information Retrieval (IR) systems. Creating a diverse set of user queries for these collections can be challenging, and obtaining relevance judgments, which indicate how well retrieved documents match a query, is often costly and resource-intensive. Recently, generating synthetic datasets using Large Language Models (LLMs) has gained attention in various applications. While previous work has used LLMs to generate synthetic queries or documents to improve ranking models, using LLMs to create synthetic test collections is still relatively unexplored. Previous work~\cite{rahmani2024synthetic} showed that synthetic test collections have the potential to be used for system evaluation, however, more analysis is needed to validate this claim. In this paper, we thoroughly investigate the reliability of synthetic test collections constructed using LLMs, where LLMs are used to generate synthetic queries, labels, or both. In particular, we examine the potential biases that might occur when such test collections are used for evaluation. We first empirically show the presence of such bias in evaluation results and analyse the effects it might have on system evaluation. We further validate the presence of such bias using a linear mixed-effects model. Our analysis shows that while the effect of bias present in evaluation results obtained using synthetic test collections could be significant, for e.g.~computing absolute system performance, its effect may not be as significant in comparing relative system performance. Codes and data are available at: https://github.com/rahmanidashti/BiasSyntheticData.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper "Towards Understanding Bias in Synthetic Data for Evaluation."

**Summary:**

The paper investigates biases present in synthetic test collections created using Large Language Models (LLMs) for information retrieval (IR) system evaluation. The authors examine biases introduced when LLMs generate queries, relevance labels, or both. Through empirical analysis, they demonstrate the presence of such biases and their effects on evaluation results.  Specifically, they find that synthetic queries tend to be longer than real user queries and that LLMs exhibit a tendency to assign higher relevance scores compared to human evaluators.  They also observe that LLM-based systems receive higher scores when evaluated on synthetic data, suggesting that synthetic test collections may overestimate system effectiveness and potentially favor systems based on similar LLMs. The analysis uses the TREC DL 2023 dataset, containing both human and synthetic data. The paper concludes that human oversight remains crucial when using LLMs for test collection construction to mitigate these biases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a relevant and timely problem: the potential biases in using LLMs to generate synthetic test collections for IR evaluation. While previous work has explored the use of LLMs for query and document generation or relevance judgments, this paper specifically focuses on analyzing and characterizing the biases within synthetic test collections when LLMs are used to generate queries and labels *simultaneously* and analyzing the effect on the relative rank of retrieval systems using LLMs. This is a valuable contribution, extending existing research on LLM-based data generation. However, the paper's core methodology relies on standard statistical techniques and data analysis methods (e.g., Bland-Altman plots, linear mixed-effects models). There isn't a substantial methodological innovation. The main novelty lies in applying these methods to this specific problem and data.

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings are significant for the IR community. As LLMs become increasingly prevalent in IR research, understanding and mitigating biases in synthetic data is crucial to ensure fair and reliable system evaluation. The paper highlights the potential pitfalls of relying solely on synthetic data, cautioning against overestimating system performance and inadvertently favoring systems aligned with the LLM used for data generation. This could significantly impact future research directions and practices in IR evaluation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly defines the research question and scope.
    *   **Comprehensive analysis:** The analysis is reasonably thorough, employing multiple methods to investigate biases in different aspects of synthetic test collections. It uses TREC DL 2023 dataset which already comes with human and synthetic components.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The results are presented clearly and concisely, with appropriate visualizations.
    *   **Practical implications:** The paper offers valuable insights and practical recommendations for researchers and practitioners working with LLMs in IR evaluation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset:** The analysis is primarily based on a *single* dataset (TREC DL 2023). Generalizing the findings to other datasets, domains, or tasks requires further validation.
    *   **Lack of Mitigation Strategies:** The paper primarily focuses on *identifying* biases but doesn't offer concrete strategies for *mitigating* them. Suggesting methods to reduce bias and make synthetic test collections more reliable would enhance the paper's practical value.
    *   **Superficial Analysis of Query Difficulty:** The paper acknowledges "query difficulty" as a factor, but it does not deeply explore how it is measured or how LLMs perceive and reflect query difficulty in their generated data. A more robust analysis of query difficulty could provide further insights into the nature of the bias.
    *   **Limited novelty in methodology:** The statistical methods (Bland-Altman plots, linear mixed-effects models) are standard and well-established. The novelty lies more in the application of these methods to this specific research question.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in IR evaluation by raising awareness of biases in LLM-based synthetic data and promoting the use of more rigorous evaluation methodologies. It may spur further research into methods for mitigating these biases.

**Score:** 6.5

**Rationale:** The paper addresses a relevant and important issue with clear analysis and significant implications for IR evaluation. It demonstrates the presence of bias in LLM-generated evaluation datasets which is valuable to the community. However, the analysis is limited by a single dataset, doesn't offer specific mitigation strategies, has relatively limited methodological innovation, and only scratches the surface of query difficulty analysis. While the findings are important, the lack of concrete solutions and the reliance on standard methods justify a slightly lower score.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Uncertainty-Aware Deep Learning for Automated Skin Cancer Classification: A Comprehensive Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10302v1)
- **Authors**: Hamzeh Asgharnezhad, Pegah Tabarisaadi, Abbas Khosravi, Roohallah Alizadehsani, U. Rajendra Acharya
- **Abstract**: Accurate and reliable skin cancer diagnosis is critical for early treatment and improved patient outcomes. Deep learning (DL) models have shown promise in automating skin cancer classification, but their performance can be limited by data scarcity and a lack of uncertainty awareness. In this study, we present a comprehensive evaluation of DL-based skin lesion classification using transfer learning and uncertainty quantification (UQ) on the HAM10000 dataset. In the first phase, we benchmarked several pre-trained feature extractors-including Contrastive Language-Image Pretraining (CLIP) variants, Residual Network-50 (ResNet50), Densely Connected Convolutional Network (DenseNet121), Visual Geometry Group network (VGG16), and EfficientNet-V2-Large-combined with a range of traditional classifiers such as Support Vector Machine (SVM), eXtreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost), and logistic regression. Our results show that CLIP-based vision transformers, particularly LAION CLIP ViT-H/14 with SVM, deliver the highest classification performance. In the second phase, we incorporated UQ using Monte Carlo Dropout (MCD), Ensemble, and Ensemble Monte Carlo Dropout (EMCD) to assess not only prediction accuracy but also the reliability of model outputs. We evaluated these models using uncertainty-aware metrics such as uncertainty accuracy(UAcc), uncertainty sensitivity(USen), uncertainty specificity(USpe), and uncertainty precision(UPre). The results demonstrate that ensemble methods offer a good trade-off between accuracy and uncertainty handling, while EMCD is more sensitive to uncertain predictions. This study highlights the importance of integrating UQ into DL-based medical diagnosis to enhance both performance and trustworthiness in real-world clinical applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation of deep learning (DL) based methods for automated skin cancer classification using the HAM10000 dataset. It focuses on two main aspects: 1) benchmarking different pre-trained feature extractors (CLIP variants, ResNet50, DenseNet121, VGG16, EfficientNet-V2-Large) combined with traditional classifiers (SVM, XGBoost, logistic regression), and 2) incorporating uncertainty quantification (UQ) using Monte Carlo Dropout (MCD), Ensemble, and Ensemble Monte Carlo Dropout (EMCD). The study evaluates these models not just on prediction accuracy but also on uncertainty-aware metrics like uncertainty accuracy (UAcc), sensitivity (USen), specificity (USpe), and precision (UPre). The results show that CLIP-based vision transformers (especially LAION CLIP ViT-H/14 with SVM) perform best, and ensemble methods offer a good accuracy/uncertainty trade-off. The authors emphasize the importance of integrating UQ into DL-based medical diagnosis to enhance performance and trustworthiness.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper makes several contributions, but its true novelty is somewhat limited. Here's a breakdown:

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper offers a fairly broad evaluation of multiple feature extraction and classification combinations. The exhaustive approach to benchmarking these different architectural choices offers immediate value and can provide a practical resource for researchers to quickly understand how different architectures are working on this dataset.
    *   **CLIP Integration:**  The exploration of CLIP (Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training) models, particularly vision transformers, is a valuable contribution. Applying these more recent models, pre-trained on very large datasets, to medical image classification is an interesting avenue to pursue.
    *   **Uncertainty Quantification (UQ):** The focus on UQ is crucial and directly addresses a key limitation of DL in medical applications. The inclusion of uncertainty-aware metrics provides a more nuanced understanding of model performance. While there's little to no method developed, providing an evaluation of how different UQ techniques works on CLIP is a strong point of this paper.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty in Methodology:** The individual components used (CLIP, SVM, UQ techniques) are not inherently novel. The paper primarily applies existing techniques rather than introducing fundamentally new methods. It appears to evaluate several combinations but does not add any unique insights or modifications to these techniques.
    *   **Limited Technical Depth:** The paper lacks detailed explanations of why certain feature-classifier combinations work better. A deeper analysis of the learned features and decision boundaries would significantly enhance the paper's scientific contribution. Without explaining the learned model, it's just a benchmarking study which is not an impactful work.
    *   **Missing Comparison to Medical-Specific Pre-Training:** While using CLIP is interesting, the paper misses out on exploring medical image-specific pre-training. Evaluating pre-trained models specifically on medical datasets would offer a better comparison and highlight the benefits (or drawbacks) of text-image contrastive learning vs. domain-specific pre-training.
    *   **Lack of Error Analysis:**  The paper doesn't offer detailed error analysis or examples of failure cases. This would provide more context for understanding the types of errors the models make and how uncertainty estimates correlate with these errors.

**Significance:**

The paper has practical significance in providing a solid benchmark for skin cancer classification with DL and highlighting the importance of UQ. The result that CLIP-based models perform well is useful information for the community. However, its limited methodological novelty reduces its overall impact. The emphasis on trustworthy AI and uncertainty estimation is valuable and aligns with current trends in medical AI.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper provides a valuable, comprehensive experimental analysis of several deep learning-based methods for skin cancer classification, with a focus on uncertainty quantification. The application of CLIP models to this domain and the integration of UQ techniques are important steps towards building more reliable medical AI systems. However, the paper's reliance on existing techniques and its lack of deeper analytical insights limit its novelty and overall contribution. It's primarily a benchmarking study, which is valuable but doesn't push the boundaries of the field significantly.

### AC/DC: LLM-based Audio Comprehension via Dialogue Continuation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10312v1)
- **Authors**: Yusuke Fujita, Tomoya Mizumoto, Atsushi Kojima, Lianbo Liu, Yui Sudo
- **Abstract**: We propose an instruction-following audio comprehension model that leverages the dialogue continuation ability of large language models (LLMs). Instead of directly generating target captions in training data, the proposed method trains a model to produce responses as if the input caption triggered a dialogue. This dialogue continuation training mitigates the caption variation problem. Learning to continue a dialogue effectively captures the caption's meaning beyond its surface-level words. As a result, our model enables zero-shot instruction-following capability without multitask instruction tuning, even trained solely on audio captioning datasets. Experiments on AudioCaps, WavCaps, and Clotho datasets with AudioBench audio-scene question-answering tests demonstrate our model's ability to follow various unseen instructions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "AC/DC: LLM-based Audio Comprehension via Dialogue Continuation" proposes a novel training method for audio comprehension models.  Instead of directly training the model to generate captions for audio, it trains the model to produce responses as if the audio caption triggered a dialogue. This "dialogue continuation training" aims to address the challenges of caption variation and data scarcity in audio captioning. By training the model to understand the *meaning* behind captions rather than just surface-level words, it aims to improve zero-shot instruction following capabilities. The paper evaluates its approach on AudioCaps, WavCaps, and Clotho datasets with AudioBench question-answering tests, demonstrating the model's ability to follow unseen instructions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using dialogue continuation training for audio comprehension is relatively novel.  Traditional methods focus on caption generation or paraphrasing. Re-framing the problem as a dialogue continuation task allows leveraging pre-trained LLMs in a different way. The approach of not directly training on target captions, but instead on generated responses based on those captions is a key distinguishing factor from existing methods. However, the architecture itself largely reuses components from SLAM-AAC, meaning much of the heavy lifting in terms of low-level audio processing and model structure isn't original. The novelty lies primarily in the *training strategy*. The use of the LLM to generate the dialogue targets, mitigating caption variability, is a clever idea.
*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is significant.  The ability to perform zero-shot instruction following on audio data would be a substantial improvement in audio understanding. The results from the experiments, which are detailed by the authors, do suggest a positive direction, even though performance is not always better than models with multiple training steps. The work moves away from task-specific training and focuses on building a more general audio understanding system, which has broader applications. The experimental setup is also significant as it leverages the current state-of-the-art tools to measure improvement across the data.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The dialogue continuation training method is an innovative approach to tackle caption variation and improve generalization.
    *   The paper demonstrates that the trained model exhibits zero-shot instruction-following capabilities without explicit multitask training.
    *   The experimental results, while not always surpassing existing models, validate the potential of the proposed approach, especially in AQA.
    *   The work aligns well with current trends in utilizing large language models for multimodal tasks.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a pre-trained LLM and audio encoder means that the model's performance is inherently tied to the capabilities of those components. It also means, for example, that if an aspect of the task is poor in the LLM, this method will also struggle.
    *   The AAC performance is slightly *worse* than baseline models, indicating a potential trade-off between task-specific performance and generalization.
    *   The paper could benefit from more in-depth analysis of the generated dialogues and how they contribute to improved instruction following.
    *   The method requires sampling to generate the target captions, making it somewhat non-deterministic and potentially introducing noise into the training process.
    *   While the overall approach is clear, the precise effect of changing each variable through dialogue creation is not clear.
*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper is likely to inspire further research into alternative training methods for audio understanding models. The dialogue continuation approach could be applied to other multimodal tasks and could lead to the development of more general and flexible audio processing systems. However, this influence will depend on follow-up studies that confirm the findings and address the weaknesses mentioned above.

**Score:** 7/10

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and promising training method that addresses a critical challenge in audio captioning and understanding. The dialogue continuation approach is innovative and demonstrates improved zero-shot instruction following. It builds on previous art, such as SLAM-AAC, but expands the scope by using a creative approach to the training targets, leading to an increased ability for the audio processor to understand a wide range of instructions. While the AAC performance isn't improved, the potential for generalization and zero-shot learning is significant. The reliance on pre-trained models and the lack of in-depth analysis of the dialogue generation process are areas for improvement. However, the potential impact of this work warrants a score of 7.

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 7/10

### ELFuzz: Efficient Input Generation via LLM-driven Synthesis Over Fuzzer Space
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10323v1)
- **Authors**: Chuyang Chen, Brendan Dolan-Gavitt, Zhiqiang Lin
- **Abstract**: Generation-based fuzzing produces appropriate testing cases according to specifications of input grammars and semantic constraints to test systems and software. However, these specifications require significant manual efforts to construct. This paper proposes a new approach, ELFuzz (Evolution Through Large Language Models for Fuzzing), that automatically synthesizes generation-based fuzzers tailored to a system under test (SUT) via LLM-driven synthesis over fuzzer space. At a high level, it starts with minimal seed fuzzers and propels the synthesis by fully automated LLM-driven evolution with coverage guidance. Compared to previous approaches, ELFuzz can 1) seamlessly scale to SUTs of real-world sizes -- up to 1,791,104 lines of code in our evaluation -- and 2) synthesize efficient fuzzers that catch interesting grammatical structures and semantic constraints in a human-understandable way. Our evaluation compared ELFuzz with specifications manually written by domain experts and synthesized by state-of-the-art approaches. It shows that ELFuzz achieves up to 434.8% more coverage and triggers up to 174.0% more artificially injected bugs. We also used ELFuzz to conduct a real-world fuzzing campaign on the newest version of cvc5 for 14 days, and encouragingly, it found five 0-day bugs (three are exploitable). Moreover, we conducted an ablation study, which shows that the fuzzer space model, the key component of ELFuzz, contributes the most (up to 62.5%) to the effectiveness of ELFuzz. Further analysis of the fuzzers synthesized by ELFuzz confirms that they catch interesting grammatical structures and semantic constraints in a human-understandable way. The results present the promising potential of ELFuzz for more automated, efficient, and extensible input generation for fuzzing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ELFuzz paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ELFuzz, a novel approach to automatically synthesizing generation-based fuzzers using a Large Language Model (LLM).  ELFuzz addresses the challenge of creating complex grammar and semantic constraints for generation-based fuzzing, which is traditionally a manual and time-consuming process. ELFuzz uses an LLM-driven evolution loop, starting with a naive seed fuzzer and iteratively improving it by mutating and evaluating candidate fuzzers. The key innovation is the "fuzzer space," a lattice structure based on code coverage that guides the LLM-driven evolution towards more effective fuzzers.  The evaluation demonstrates that ELFuzz can scale to large, real-world systems, achieve significant coverage gains compared to existing techniques, and discover new bugs in real-world software (cvc5). Ablation studies highlight the importance of the fuzzer space guidance. The paper also shows initial steps to demonstrate the interpretability and extensibility of ELFuzz’s approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The concept of "fuzzer space" as a structured way to guide LLM-based fuzzer synthesis is a genuine contribution. Using LLMs for fuzzing isn't entirely new, but ELFuzz's specific evolution loop, guided by fuzzer space, and the focus on *generating* fuzzers rather than just individual inputs is a distinguishing factor. The LLM-driven evolution that divides the fuzzer synthesis task into small steps to overcome the limitations of coding capability of the LLM is novel.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a crucial bottleneck in fuzzing: the difficulty of creating effective generation-based fuzzers.  By automating this process, ELFuzz could make generation-based fuzzing more accessible and applicable to a wider range of systems. The results demonstrating its ability to find new bugs in a complex system like cvc5 are significant, showing that it can go beyond toy examples.  If ELFuzz can be generalized and scaled further, it could have a substantial impact on software security.  The approach is also interpretable and extensible, which is of value.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Practical Scalability:**  The demonstration of ELFuzz on codebases with millions of lines of code is a key strength.
    * **Effective Bug Finding:** The discovery of real-world bugs in cvc5 is a strong validation of the approach.
    * **Well-Designed Evaluation:** The controlled experiments with bug injection and comparisons to relevant baselines are well-executed.  Ablation studies help to isolate the impact of individual components.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and explains the key concepts (fuzzer space, LLM-driven evolution) clearly.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **LLM dependency:** As with any LLM-based approach, ELFuzz's performance depends on the capabilities of the LLM used. Future evolution in LLMs is likely to boost the performance, but there’s also a risk for data contamination, or a lack of data to help the LLM. The approach also presents more challenges when dealing with SUT with binary input formats.
    * **Generalizability to all input formats:** While interpretable and extensible, the approach has certain limitations. It’s not as generalizable to all input formats. Uncommon SUTs may also cause problems.
    * **Evolution Time vs. Effectiveness:** The trade-off between the time spent synthesizing fuzzers and the resulting fuzzing effectiveness needs further investigation.  Although there were experiments to explore this direction, it is still preliminary. Also, there are experiments to show potential future speed-up, which may be beneficial.
    * **Limited Generalization:** The case studies provide evidence for interpretability and extensibility, but they are limited in scope. More diverse case studies would strengthen the claims.
    * **Runtime Overhead of Generated Fuzzers:** Although the paper mentions the elimination of runtime overhead caused by grammar rule instantiation, there is not enough data or discussion regarding the actual runtime performance of the generated python scripts.

* **Potential Impact:**  ELFuzz has the potential to:
    * Lower the barrier to entry for generation-based fuzzing.
    * Improve the effectiveness of fuzzing by automating the creation of customized fuzzers.
    * Facilitate the discovery of vulnerabilities in complex systems that are difficult to test with traditional fuzzing techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

ELFuzz presents a significant contribution to the field of fuzzing by tackling the challenging problem of automated input generation. The "fuzzer space" concept provides a novel and structured approach to guide LLM-driven synthesis, resulting in demonstrably effective fuzzers that can scale to real-world systems and uncover new bugs. While the approach has certain limitations related to LLM dependency and generalizability, the strengths significantly outweigh the weaknesses. Therefore, I would give this paper a score of **8**.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Augmenting Large Language Models with Static Code Analysis for Automated Code Quality Improvements
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10330v1)
- **Authors**: Seyed Moein Abtahi, Akramul Azim
- **Abstract**: This study examined code issue detection and revision automation by integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) such as OpenAI's GPT-3.5 Turbo and GPT-4o into software development workflows. A static code analysis framework detects issues such as bugs, vulnerabilities, and code smells within a large-scale software project. Detailed information on each issue was extracted and organized to facilitate automated code revision using LLMs. An iterative prompt engineering process is applied to ensure that prompts are structured to produce accurate and organized outputs aligned with the project requirements. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is implemented to enhance the relevance and precision of the revisions, enabling LLM to access and integrate real-time external knowledge. The issue of LLM hallucinations - where the model generates plausible but incorrect outputs - is addressed by a custom-built "Code Comparison App," which identifies and corrects erroneous changes before applying them to the codebase. Subsequent scans using the static code analysis framework revealed a significant reduction in code issues, demonstrating the effectiveness of combining LLMs, static analysis, and RAG to improve code quality, streamline the software development process, and reduce time and resource expenditure.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically OpenAI's GPT-3.5 Turbo and GPT-4o, in automating code quality improvements through static code analysis. The authors integrate LLMs with a static analysis framework to detect bugs, vulnerabilities, and code smells. They utilize prompt engineering and Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) to enhance the accuracy and relevance of code revisions. A custom-built "Code Comparison App" is introduced to address LLM hallucinations. The study demonstrates a reduction in code issues after applying the combined approach, suggesting that LLMs, static analysis, and RAG can improve code quality and streamline development. The paper also compares a divided approach (addressing issues by category) with a comprehensive approach (addressing all issues in one prompt).  Finally, it mentions the application of LLMs into CI/CD pipelines as future work.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The integration of LLMs with static code analysis isn't entirely novel, as other works have explored similar concepts (cited in the paper). The specific contribution lies in the comprehensive evaluation using GPT-3.5 Turbo and GPT-4o, the implementation of a custom hallucination detection tool, and the comparison of different prompting strategies (divided vs. comprehensive). However, many sections of the methodology, like retrieval of information from external resources using RAG or Prompt Engineering are already known. Also, the idea of using LLMs in automated code repair or CI/CD pipelines are present in prior work as well. The limited descriptions of the custom code comparison application make it difficult to assess its novelty beyond simply flagging differences.

*   **Significance:** The paper provides empirical evidence supporting the potential benefits of LLMs in code quality assurance. The reduction in code issues after LLM-assisted revision is a significant finding. The comparison of prompting strategies and the observation that a divided approach is superior contributes to a better understanding of how to effectively utilize LLMs for code improvement.  Furthermore, the analysis of the cost-effectiveness of different LLMs (GPT-3.5 Turbo vs. GPT-4o) is valuable for practical application. The inclusion of the Code Comparison App improves trust and reduce the potential issues with hallucination, helping to increase the reliability of the findings.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear methodology and experimental setup.
    *   Quantitative evaluation using precision, recall, and F1-score.
    *   Comparison of different LLMs and prompting strategies.
    *   Addresses a practical problem in software development.
    *   Code Comparison App for addressing LLM hallucination
    *   Analyzes RAG impact on performance

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited detail on the implementation of the "Code Comparison App." It would be beneficial to know more about its mechanism and how it detects hallucination beyond just showing differences.
    *   The dataset and code base used (the "EIS" project) are not extensively described, limiting the generalizability of the results.
    *   The paper does not address the scalability constraints and potential issues related to API costs with very large codebases and a large number of calls.
    *   The paper lacks a rigorous comparison of the RAG apprach in comparison to the model by itself.
    *   The cost difference between LLMs are very limited and do not correspond to the reality.

*   **Potential Influence:** The findings can influence software development practices by encouraging the adoption of LLM-assisted code review and improvement tools. The insights into prompting strategies and the benefits of a divided approach can guide developers in effectively using LLMs. The hallucination correction is useful.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a reasonable contribution by providing a comprehensive evaluation of LLMs for code quality improvement. However, the novelty is somewhat limited, and the description of some key components (the "Code Comparison App") is insufficient. The scope is somewhat limited as it only considers code smells, bug and vulnerabilities.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### GeoCAD: Local Geometry-Controllable CAD Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10337v1)
- **Authors**: Zhanwei Zhang, Kaiyuan Liu, Junjie Liu, Wenxiao Wang, Binbin Lin, Liang Xie, Chen Shen, Deng Cai
- **Abstract**: Local geometry-controllable computer-aided design (CAD) generation aims to modify local parts of CAD models automatically, enhancing design efficiency. It also ensures that the shapes of newly generated local parts follow user-specific geometric instructions (e.g., an isosceles right triangle or a rectangle with one corner cut off). However, existing methods encounter challenges in achieving this goal. Specifically, they either lack the ability to follow textual instructions or are unable to focus on the local parts. To address this limitation, we introduce GeoCAD, a user-friendly and local geometry-controllable CAD generation method. Specifically, we first propose a complementary captioning strategy to generate geometric instructions for local parts. This strategy involves vertex-based and VLLM-based captioning for systematically annotating simple and complex parts, respectively. In this way, we caption $\sim$221k different local parts in total. In the training stage, given a CAD model, we randomly mask a local part. Then, using its geometric instruction and the remaining parts as input, we prompt large language models (LLMs) to predict the masked part. During inference, users can specify any local part for modification while adhering to a variety of predefined geometric instructions. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of GeoCAD in generation quality, validity and text-to-CAD consistency. Code will be available at https://github.com/Zhanwei-Z/GeoCAD.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GeoCAD: Local Geometry-Controllable CAD Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GeoCAD, a novel method for local geometry-controllable CAD generation. It addresses the limitations of existing methods that either struggle to follow textual instructions for shape modification or fail to focus on specific local parts of a CAD model. GeoCAD uses a complementary captioning strategy consisting of vertex-based captioning for simple shapes and VLLM-based captioning for complex shapes to generate geometric instructions for local parts. This enables a two-stage LLM fine-tuning process. During training, a local part of a CAD model is randomly masked, and the LLM is prompted to predict the masked part using the geometric instruction and the remaining parts as context.  At inference time, users can specify any local part for modification and provide geometric instructions, which the trained model then uses to generate the new shape. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of GeoCAD in generating high-quality, valid, and textually consistent CAD models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper demonstrates a few key novelties:
*   **Local Geometry Control:** While previous methods focus on generating entire CAD models from text or modifying sketch-level attributes, GeoCAD explicitly targets local geometry manipulation guided by text. This is a significant and practical advancement.
*   **Complementary Captioning Strategy:** The paper introduces a smart captioning strategy that combines vertex-based and VLLM-based techniques that are designed specifically to address the lack of high-quality training data for CAD loops. That vertex-based captioning specifically and successfully addresses the shortcomings of VLLMs on simple geometric objects contributes to the novelty of the approach.
*   **LLM Fine-tuning for Local Manipulation:** Utilizing a two-stage fine-tuning approach, the paper effectively guides LLMs to understand and generate CAD model representations, specifically emphasizing local geometry constraints. The combination of this technique with captioning is novel.

**Significance:**

The significance lies in the potential of GeoCAD to enhance design efficiency and user control in CAD modeling. The ability to precisely modify local parts based on textual instructions opens possibilities for faster prototyping, design iteration, and personalized CAD model generation.
*   **Practical Impact:** The method has clear practical implications for CAD users, allowing them to easily express design intentions for specific parts and automate the generation of complex CAD models.
*   **Advancement of Text-to-CAD:** The paper significantly advances the text-to-CAD field by addressing a specific and relevant problem: local geometry manipulation. It builds upon recent advances in LLMs and contributes novel techniques for CAD-specific tasks.
*   **Community Benefit:** The introduction of GeoCAD will allow CAD designers to design more quickly and effectively.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the limitations of existing CAD generation methods and motivates the need for local geometry control.
*   **Well-Defined Method:** The proposed GeoCAD method is described in detail, including the captioning strategy, LLM fine-tuning process, and inference procedure.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes thorough experimental validation, using a standard dataset (DeepCAD) and several metrics to evaluate generation quality, validity, and text-to-CAD consistency. Qualitative results provide further insights into the model's capabilities.
*   **Strong Results:** The experimental results demonstrate that GeoCAD outperforms existing baselines (OpenAI and FlexCAD) on various metrics.
*   **Practical System.** By successfully fine-tuning LLMs to act as CAD design interfaces, this paper provides a significant contribution that will be utilized by the CAD community.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependency on DeepCAD:** The evaluation is based solely on the DeepCAD dataset, which might limit the generalizability of the findings. Evaluation on more diverse CAD datasets or real-world design scenarios would strengthen the paper.
*   **Complexity of Captioning Pipeline:** While the complementary captioning strategy is a key contribution, it adds complexity to the overall system. The trade-off between performance gains and increased complexity should be further discussed.
*   **Implicit part relationships not addressed:** As the authors themselves identify, The DeepCAD dataset does not describe relationships between parts. Future work to remedy this limitation will improve the usefulness of GeoCAD.
*   **No ablation study of LoRA rank/alpha** The authors mentioned they followed the same LoRA settings as FelxCAD. It is unfortunate that there is no mention of how important LoRA settings could be to final performance and stability.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of CAD generation. It addresses a practical and relevant problem, introduces innovative techniques (the captioning strategy), and provides strong experimental evidence to support its claims. The paper's weaknesses, such as the dependency on a single dataset and the complexity of the captioning pipeline, are relatively minor compared to its strengths. The future work described in the paper also outlines a concrete path to even further improvements.
The paper's approach successfully fine-tunes the use of LLMs for CAD applications.

Score: 7.8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### UrbanSense:AFramework for Quantitative Analysis of Urban Streetscapes leveraging Vision Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10342v1)
- **Authors**: Jun Yin, Jing Zhong, Peilin Li, Pengyu Zeng, Miao Zhang, Ran Luo, Shuai Lu
- **Abstract**: Urban cultures and architectural styles vary significantly across cities due to geographical, chronological, historical, and socio-political factors. Understanding these differences is essential for anticipating how cities may evolve in the future. As representative cases of historical continuity and modern innovation in China, Beijing and Shenzhen offer valuable perspectives for exploring the transformation of urban streetscapes. However, conventional approaches to urban cultural studies often rely on expert interpretation and historical documentation, which are difficult to standardize across different contexts. To address this, we propose a multimodal research framework based on vision-language models, enabling automated and scalable analysis of urban streetscape style differences. This approach enhances the objectivity and data-driven nature of urban form research. The contributions of this study are as follows: First, we construct UrbanDiffBench, a curated dataset of urban streetscapes containing architectural images from different periods and regions. Second, we develop UrbanSense, the first vision-language-model-based framework for urban streetscape analysis, enabling the quantitative generation and comparison of urban style representations. Third, experimental results show that Over 80% of generated descriptions pass the t-test (p less than 0.05). High Phi scores (0.912 for cities, 0.833 for periods) from subjective evaluations confirm the method's ability to capture subtle stylistic differences. These results highlight the method's potential to quantify and interpret urban style evolution, offering a scientifically grounded lens for future design.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "UrbanSense: A Framework for Quantitative Analysis of Urban Streetscapes leveraging Vision Large Language Models."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UrbanSense, a multimodal research framework that leverages vision-language models (VLLMs) to quantitatively analyze urban streetscape style differences. It addresses the limitations of traditional urban cultural studies that rely on expert interpretation and historical documentation. The framework includes: (1) UrbanDiffBench, a curated dataset of urban streetscapes from different periods and regions (Beijing and Shenzhen); (2) the UrbanSense framework itself, which uses VLLMs to generate and compare urban style representations. The authors demonstrate that the generated descriptions are statistically significant and capture subtle stylistic differences through both automated metrics and subjective evaluations. The work aims to offer a more objective and data-driven approach to understanding urban style evolution, providing a scientifically grounded lens for future design.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper has some elements of novelty, although the core ideas build upon existing research. The application of VLLMs to urban streetscape analysis is a relatively new area, and the development of UrbanDiffBench adds a valuable resource to the field. The core contribution lies in the specific combination of existing techniques and the construction of a tailored dataset. The methodology leverages the strengths of VLMs for semantic understanding and links it to urban morphology research. However, it's important to acknowledge that VLMs themselves are rapidly evolving, and the specific architectures (LLaVA, GPT-4V, BLIP-2) used in this paper might become outdated relatively quickly.  A central challenge is whether the proposed pipeline provides significant advantages over simply using these VLMs in a straightforward manner, which the paper does not convincingly argue.

**Significance:**

The potential significance of the paper is moderate. If the approach is robust and generalizable, it could offer urban planners and designers a more objective and data-driven way to understand urban style and its evolution. The ability to quantify stylistic differences could inform design decisions, heritage preservation, and urban renewal strategies. However, the current study is limited to two Chinese cities (Beijing and Shenzhen). The generalizability of the framework to other urban contexts needs to be rigorously tested. Furthermore, the paper doesn't explore the practical applications of this framework in real-world urban planning or design scenarios, leaving its actual impact unclear. The lack of comparisons to previous techniques in streetscape analysis and urban morphology also diminishes the perceived significance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of traditional methods in urban cultural studies.
*   **Well-Defined Framework:** UrbanSense is presented as a structured and modular framework.
*   **Curated Dataset:** The creation of UrbanDiffBench is a valuable contribution.
*   **Quantifiable Results:** The paper presents experimental results with statistical significance.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability:** The study is limited to two Chinese cities, raising questions about its applicability to other urban contexts.
*   **Lack of Application:** The paper lacks concrete examples of how the framework can be used in real-world urban planning or design scenarios.
*   **VLM Dependence:** The results are heavily dependent on the specific VLMs used, which are subject to rapid change and improvement in the field.
*   **Overstated Claims:** The claims of a "scientifically grounded lens" for urban style evolution may be slightly overstated, as the method still relies on the inherent biases and limitations of the VLM.
*   **Lack of Comparison:** The lack of comparison to the prior art methods in the field limits the ability to assess the true utility of this paper's pipeline.

**Justification for the Score:**

Given the points above, I'm assigning a score of **6**. The paper presents a moderately novel application of existing VLLMs to an interesting problem. The creation of the dataset UrbanDiffBench is a plus. However, the limited generalizability, the reliance on rapidly evolving VLM technology, the lack of practical application examples, and the lack of comparison to the prior art methods limit the significance and impact of the work. The work shows promise but requires further validation and extension to demonstrate its true potential.
Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Code Execution as Grounded Supervision for LLM Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10343v1)
- **Authors**: Dongwon Jung, Wenxuan Zhou, Muhao Chen
- **Abstract**: Training large language models (LLMs) with chain-of-thought (CoT) supervision has proven effective for enhancing their reasoning abilities. However, obtaining reliable and accurate reasoning supervision remains a significant challenge. We propose a scalable method for generating a high-quality CoT supervision dataset by leveraging the determinism of program execution. Unlike existing reasoning dataset generation methods that rely on costly human annotations or error-prone LLM-generated CoT, our approach extracts verifiable, step-by-step reasoning traces from code execution and transforms them into a natural language CoT reasoning. Experiments on reasoning benchmarks across various domains show that our method effectively equips LLMs with transferable reasoning abilities across diverse tasks. Furthermore, the ablation studies validate that our method produces highly accurate reasoning data and reduces overall token length during inference by reducing meaningless repetition and overthinking.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper. Here's a summary and a critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel method for generating high-quality Chain-of-Thought (CoT) data to supervise the reasoning process of Large Language Models (LLMs).  Unlike existing methods that rely on costly human annotations or error-prone LLM-generated CoTs, this approach leverages the determinism of program execution. The method extracts verifiable, step-by-step reasoning traces from code execution, translates these traces into natural language using LLMs, and then uses this data to fine-tune the LLMs. The key idea is that code execution provides inherently correct and causally linked reasoning steps, offering a reliable source of supervision. The paper presents experiments on reasoning benchmarks across various domains, demonstrating the effectiveness of the approach in equipping LLMs with transferable reasoning abilities. Ablation studies validate the accuracy of the reasoning data and the reduction in token length during inference due to minimized repetition and overthinking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of using code execution traces as a grounded source of CoT data is quite novel. While using code for reasoning has been explored before, this paper's approach of extracting execution traces and then translating them into natural language CoT for general LLM reasoning supervision seems unique.  The novelty lies in the *combination* of code execution for correctness *and* LLMs for natural language expression of reasoning. The method circumvents the known issues with LLM-generated rationales by ensuring grounding in code execution. The combination of Snoop (a debugging tool) and LLM to translate the trace to a human-readable format also contributes to novelty.

**Significance:**  The significance is moderate to high.  Generating high-quality, scalable, and accurate CoT data is a major bottleneck in LLM research. This method addresses this challenge by providing an annotation-free pipeline that is inherently more reliable than LLM-generated CoT or human-annotated data (which is expensive).  The demonstrated improvement in performance across various reasoning benchmarks suggests the practical utility of the approach.  The method has potential for broader impact in areas where problems can be formalized and solved with executable code, which covers a significant portion of reasoning tasks. The reduction in token length during inference, reported in ablation studies, is also a relevant contribution to the efficiency of LLM reasoning.

**Strengths:**

*   **Verifiable Reasoning:** The primary strength is the guaranteed correctness of the underlying reasoning steps, derived from code execution.
*   **Scalability:** The method is scalable since it relies on automated code execution and translation, rather than human annotations.
*   **Transferability:** The experiments demonstrate transferability of reasoning abilities across diverse tasks.
*   **Efficiency:**  The ablation studies indicate improved data quality and reduced token length, leading to efficiency gains during inference.
*   **Well-documented experiments:** Clear experimental setup, well-defined baselines, and comprehensive evaluation metrics.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Domain Limitation:** The most significant limitation is that the method is restricted to problems that can be formalized and solved with executable code. This excludes tasks that rely on common sense, social intelligence, or subjective knowledge.
*   **Translation Imperfection:** The reliance on an LLM for translating execution traces introduces a potential source of error, although the authors claim high accuracy in translation and validate it using OpenAI-03.
*   **Code Availability:** The method depends on the availability of solution code. While there is an increasing amount of code available for various tasks, this may not be the case for all reasoning problems. Although, the study uses existing solutions and does not require the models to come up with the solution.
*   **Complexity of Code:** Complex or poorly written code could lead to convoluted execution traces that are difficult to translate into clear CoT.
*   **Potential for Overfitting:** While the results show generalizability, training solely on execution-based reasoning could lead to overfitting to the type of reasoning inherent in code.

**Justification of Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7.5**.

Here's the rationale:

*   The **novelty is significant**. The idea of grounding CoT generation in code execution traces and then translating this into natural language is a clever approach to bypass limitations of standard LLM CoT methods.
*   The **significance is moderate to high**.  The paper tackles a major problem in LLM research, CoT data quality and generation, and the results demonstrate tangible improvements in LLM reasoning across tasks. The scalability aspect contributes further to its potential impact.
*   The **weaknesses, however, cannot be ignored**. The biggest issue is the domain restriction of tasks expressible in code. While the study shows usefulness for math and logical tasks, this inherently limits its applicability. Translation quality adds another potential hurdle.

A score of 7.5 reflects a solid, well-executed contribution with a novel approach that provides a practical solution to an important problem, while acknowledging that it does have inherent limitations. If the method could be adapted to other forms of grounding (e.g., knowledge graphs, formal logic representations) its score would be increased.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Motion-R1: Chain-of-Thought Reasoning and Reinforcement Learning for Human Motion Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10353v1)
- **Authors**: Runqi Ouyang, Haoyun Li, Zhenyuan Zhang, Xiaofeng Wang, Zheng Zhu, Guan Huang, Xingang Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models, especially in natural language understanding and reasoning, have opened new possibilities for text-to-motion generation. Although existing approaches have made notable progress in semantic alignment and motion synthesis, they often rely on end-to-end mapping strategies that fail to capture deep linguistic structures and logical reasoning. Consequently, generated motions tend to lack controllability, consistency, and diversity. To address these limitations, we propose Motion-R1, a unified motion-language modeling framework that integrates a Chain-of-Thought mechanism. By explicitly decomposing complex textual instructions into logically structured action paths, Motion-R1 provides high-level semantic guidance for motion generation, significantly enhancing the model's ability to interpret and execute multi-step, long-horizon, and compositionally rich commands. To train our model, we adopt Group Relative Policy Optimization, a reinforcement learning algorithm designed for large models, which leverages motion quality feedback to optimize reasoning chains and motion synthesis jointly. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmark datasets demonstrate that Motion-R1 achieves competitive or superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods, particularly in scenarios requiring nuanced semantic understanding and long-term temporal coherence. The code, model and data will be publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Motion-R1: Chain-of-Thought Reasoning and Reinforcement Learning for Human Motion Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Motion-R1, a novel framework for text-to-motion (T2M) generation. It tackles the limitations of existing end-to-end approaches that struggle with complex instructions, lacking deep linguistic understanding and logical reasoning. Motion-R1 integrates a Chain-of-Thought (CoT) mechanism to decompose complex textual inputs into structured action plans. It then employs Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), a reinforcement learning technique, to optimize the reasoning chains and motion synthesis based on motion quality feedback. The authors also introduce MotionCoT Data Engine, an automated pipeline that leverages LLMs to generate high-quality CoT annotations for training. Experimental results on benchmark datasets demonstrate that Motion-R1 achieves competitive or superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods, particularly in scenarios demanding nuanced semantic understanding and long-term temporal coherence.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its integrated approach of combining CoT reasoning with reinforcement learning in the T2M domain. While CoT has been explored in other areas and RL has seen limited use in T2M, the specific combination and the MotionCoT Data Engine offer a new perspective. GRPO is a relatively recent RL algorithm, and its application to T2M is also novel. However, the individual components (CoT, GRPO, VQ-VAE) are not entirely new, and their application to T2M is an extension of existing techniques. The motion tokenizer is a standard VQ-VAE.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work comes from addressing a critical limitation of existing T2M systems: the inability to handle complex instructions requiring reasoning and planning. By explicitly modeling the reasoning process, Motion-R1 improves the controllability, consistency, and diversity of generated motions. The MotionCoT Data Engine has the potential to reduce the reliance on manual annotation, making the training of T2M models more scalable. The use of GRPO presents a computationally efficient alternative to other RL methods that often involve value networks or human preferences. Demonstrating improvements on standard benchmarks further strengthens its significance. A key strength is the qualitative results showcasing superior handling of both complex and abstract instructions, a clear indicator of improved reasoning capabilities.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated approach.
    *   Integration of CoT and RL offers a promising solution to improve T2M.
    *   MotionCoT Data Engine automates the annotation process.
    *   GRPO is a computationally efficient RL method.
    *   Strong experimental results on standard benchmarks.
    *   Qualitative results demonstrate the model's ability to handle complex and abstract descriptions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The individual components (CoT, GRPO, VQ-VAE) are not fundamentally new.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the limitations of GRPO in this context and how future work might address them.
    *   Dependency on the API from DeepSeekAI and Qwen may limit reproducibility and accessibility.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of T2M by encouraging researchers to explore reasoning-based approaches. The MotionCoT Data Engine could become a valuable tool for generating training data. The use of GRPO may also inspire the development of other efficient RL algorithms for T2M. Future work could build upon Motion-R1 by investigating different CoT strategies, reward functions, and RL algorithms. The framework's modularity will likely facilitate future adaptations and improvements.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed integration of existing techniques to address a significant limitation in the T2M domain. While the individual components are not groundbreaking on their own, the combination is innovative and effective. The performance improvements and qualitative results support the claim that Motion-R1 advances the state-of-the-art. The automated CoT data generation aspect and the efficient RL approach are strong positives. However, the reliance on specific LLM APIs (which might change over time), combined with the incremental nature of the innovation (assembling existing methods) hold the score back from being higher. A score of 7 reflects a significant and promising contribution with the potential to influence future research in T2M.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### TreeLoRA: Efficient Continual Learning via Layer-Wise LoRAs Guided by a Hierarchical Gradient-Similarity Tree
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10355v1)
- **Authors**: Yu-Yang Qian, Yuan-Ze Xu, Zhen-Yu Zhang, Peng Zhao, Zhi-Hua Zhou
- **Abstract**: Many real-world applications collect data in a streaming environment, where learning tasks are encountered sequentially. This necessitates continual learning (CL) to update models online, enabling adaptation to new tasks while preserving past knowledge to prevent catastrophic forgetting. Nowadays, with the flourish of large pre-trained models (LPMs), efficiency has become increasingly critical for CL, due to their substantial computational demands and growing parameter sizes. In this paper, we introduce TreeLoRA (K-D Tree of Low-Rank Adapters), a novel approach that constructs layer-wise adapters by leveraging hierarchical gradient similarity to enable efficient CL, particularly for LPMs. To reduce the computational burden of task similarity estimation, we employ bandit techniques to develop an algorithm based on lower confidence bounds to efficiently explore the task structure. Furthermore, we use sparse gradient updates to facilitate parameter optimization, making the approach better suited for LPMs. Theoretical analysis is provided to justify the rationale behind our approach, and experiments on both vision transformers (ViTs) and large language models (LLMs) demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of our approach across various domains, including vision and natural language processing tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the TreeLoRA paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "TreeLoRA: Efficient Continual Learning via Layer-Wise LoRAs Guided by a Hierarchical Gradient-Similarity Tree" proposes a new method for efficient continual learning, particularly for large pre-trained models (LPMs). The method, called TreeLoRA, constructs a hierarchical tree of layer-wise LoRA adapters, guided by the similarity of gradients observed during training. This tree structure is designed to organize and manage historical tasks efficiently, enabling the model to adapt to new tasks while preserving knowledge from previous ones. The method uses bandit techniques with lower confidence bounds to efficiently explore the task structure and sparse gradient updates for parameter optimization, making it well-suited for LPMs. The authors provide theoretical analysis and empirical results on vision transformers (ViTs) and large language models (LLMs) demonstrating the effectiveness and efficiency of their approach compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a hierarchical tree based on gradient similarity to manage LoRA adapters is a valuable contribution. It moves beyond simply adding LoRA adapters sequentially for each task. The integration of bandit algorithms for adaptive tree construction and sparse updates further enhance the novelty. The combination of these techniques distinguishes it from existing approaches. This is a significant improvement upon naive LoRA adaptation.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in the field of continual learning: the need for efficient adaptation of increasingly large models. The demonstrated speedups compared to other methods are significant and address practical concerns, especially for deploying CL solutions in real-world applications with resource constraints. The fact that the approach is applicable to both ViTs and LLMs adds to its significance. The paper has the potential to influence future research on efficient continual learning, encouraging the development of methods that effectively leverage task relationships.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The proposed method addresses an important problem of scaling CL to LPMs.
    *   The use of hierarchical gradient similarity and bandit techniques is technically sound and novel.
    *   Theoretical analysis provides a justification for the approach.
    *   The empirical results are strong, demonstrating significant speedups with comparable or improved performance.
    *   The method is applicable to different model architectures (ViTs and LLMs).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth comparison with other hierarchical or task-clustering CL methods. While the introduction touches on existing approaches, it would be strengthened with a more explicit contrast highlighting the specific advantages of TreeLoRA.
    *   The choice of the L1-norm for gradient similarity needs a more robust justification. Although the authors mentioned that L1-norm is more robust in high-dimensional spaces (Aggarwal et al., 2001), it would be good to discuss and compare other similarity measurement methods.
    *   The practical implementation details, while provided, could be expanded. Discussion surrounding the implementation of the low-rank sparsify function and the automatic tuning of the task-similarity threshold δ could provide additional clarity.
    *   The ablation study is limited. While it confirms the contribution of key components, a more comprehensive study exploring the impact of different tree depths and other parameters would be beneficial.
    *   The paper could explore the impact of changing task distribution over time, where the learned task structure may not be valid anymore. Further discussion around the robustness of the proposed approach in such scenarios could provide additional value.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence research on efficient continual learning, particularly in the context of large models. The approach of using hierarchical structures to manage knowledge and bandit algorithms for adaptive learning could inspire new methods for CL and other related fields.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** TreeLoRA presents a novel and significant approach to efficient continual learning. The hierarchical structure and bandit algorithm are technically sound and well-motivated. While there are some weaknesses in terms of depth of comparison with existing approaches and certain implementation details, the strengths of the paper, particularly the strong empirical results and the practical relevance of the problem addressed, justify a high score. The paper has the potential to have a significant impact on the field.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Optimus-3: Towards Generalist Multimodal Minecraft Agents with Scalable Task Experts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10357v1)
- **Authors**: Zaijing Li, Yuquan Xie, Rui Shao, Gongwei Chen, Weili Guan, Dongmei Jiang, Liqiang Nie
- **Abstract**: Recently, agents based on multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved remarkable progress across various domains. However, building a generalist agent with capabilities such as perception, planning, action, grounding, and reflection in open-world environments like Minecraft remains challenges: insufficient domain-specific data, interference among heterogeneous tasks, and visual diversity in open-world settings. In this paper, we address these challenges through three key contributions. 1) We propose a knowledge-enhanced data generation pipeline to provide scalable and high-quality training data for agent development. 2) To mitigate interference among heterogeneous tasks, we introduce a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture with task-level routing. 3) We develop a Multimodal Reasoning-Augmented Reinforcement Learning approach to enhance the agent's reasoning ability for visual diversity in Minecraft. Built upon these innovations, we present Optimus-3, a general-purpose agent for Minecraft. Extensive experimental results demonstrate that Optimus-3 surpasses both generalist multimodal large language models and existing state-of-the-art agents across a wide range of tasks in the Minecraft environment. Project page: https://cybertronagent.github.io/Optimus-3.github.io/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Optimus-3 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Optimus-3, a general-purpose multimodal agent designed for the open-world environment of Minecraft. The agent is equipped with perception, planning, action execution, grounding, and reflection capabilities. The paper addresses challenges such as a lack of domain-specific training data, interference among heterogeneous tasks, and visual diversity in open-world settings. The authors propose a knowledge-enhanced data generation pipeline for creating high-quality training data. They also introduce a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture with task-level routing to minimize interference among different tasks. Further, a Multimodal Reasoning-Augmented Reinforcement Learning approach improves the agent's reasoning ability in visually diverse environments. Experimental results demonstrate that Optimus-3 outperforms existing state-of-the-art agents and general-purpose multimodal models across various Minecraft tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Capabilities:** Optimus-3 brings together a suite of capabilities (perception, planning, action, grounding, and reflection) that are often treated separately in existing Minecraft agents. This integration is a significant step towards creating more versatile and human-like agents.
    *   **Data Generation Pipeline:** The knowledge-enhanced data generation pipeline is a strong contribution, particularly addressing the scarcity of domain-specific data.  The automated generation process with Minecraft-specific tools and environment feedback is a scalable and cost-effective approach.
    *   **MoE Architecture:** The task-level routing MoE architecture provides a practical solution for mitigating interference in multi-task learning. It is particularly valuable in environments like Minecraft with a variety of input/output formats. The design promotes scalability and task expansion.
    *   **Multimodal Reasoning-Augmented RL:** The approach explicitly encourages the agent to reason based on visual content, improving the interpretability of responses and enhancing performance.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The results clearly demonstrate Optimus-3's superiority over existing methods and the positive impact of each proposed component. The ablations studies are particularly important in providing evidence for these advantages.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty in Core Concepts:**  While the *combination* of approaches is novel and effective, many of the individual components (MoE, RL, data generation) are built upon existing techniques. The data generation pipelines are similar in spirit to prior work by others.
    *   **Environment Bias:** The agent is specifically designed and evaluated for Minecraft.  While Minecraft is a complex and challenging environment, the extent to which the agent's architecture can be generalized to other open-world environments remains unclear. How transferrable the framework is outside of Minecraft needs further study.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The paper represents a valuable step forward, but the core innovation builds and expands upon prior research from the same group (Optimus-1, Optimus-2), limiting the groundbreaking nature of the current work.
    *   **Lack of Zero-Shot Performance:** The paper does not explicitly discuss zero-shot generalization to unseen tasks or variations in the Minecraft environment. While in the data pipeline some variation is used in generating the data, zero-shot transfer needs to be tested.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The work contributes a valuable approach for building more capable generalist agents in complex virtual environments.
    *   The data generation pipeline can be adapted for other domains where labeled data is scarce.
    *   The MoE architecture provides a pathway for scaling agents to handle a wider range of tasks without significant interference.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper can inspire further research on multimodal reasoning and reinforcement learning in open-world environments.
    *   The data generation techniques could be adopted by other researchers working on similar problems.
    *   The task-level routing MoE design could influence the development of more scalable multi-task learning architectures.

**Justification for Score:**

Optimus-3 represents a significant step forward in creating generalist agents for complex environments like Minecraft. The combined approach of knowledge-enhanced data generation, a task-level routing MoE architecture, and multimodal reasoning-augmented RL has proven to be effective in achieving strong performance across diverse tasks. While the individual components build upon existing techniques, the integration and customization for the Minecraft environment, along with strong experimental results, make a valuable contribution to the field. The limitations regarding environmental bias and novelty in core concepts are present, so a high score is not warranted.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can We Infer Confidential Properties of Training Data from LLMs?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10364v1)
- **Authors**: Penguin Huang, Chhavi Yadav, Ruihan Wu, Kamalika Chaudhuri
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly fine-tuned on domain-specific datasets to support applications in fields such as healthcare, finance, and law. These fine-tuning datasets often have sensitive and confidential dataset-level properties -- such as patient demographics or disease prevalence -- that are not intended to be revealed. While prior work has studied property inference attacks on discriminative models (e.g., image classification models) and generative models (e.g., GANs for image data), it remains unclear if such attacks transfer to LLMs. In this work, we introduce PropInfer, a benchmark task for evaluating property inference in LLMs under two fine-tuning paradigms: question-answering and chat-completion. Built on the ChatDoctor dataset, our benchmark includes a range of property types and task configurations. We further propose two tailored attacks: a prompt-based generation attack and a shadow-model attack leveraging word frequency signals. Empirical evaluations across multiple pretrained LLMs show the success of our attacks, revealing a previously unrecognized vulnerability in LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Can We Infer Confidential Properties of Training Data from LLMs?":

**Summary:**

This paper introduces "PropInfer", a benchmark task designed to evaluate the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to property inference attacks. These attacks aim to extract dataset-level properties (e.g., patient demographics, disease prevalence) from fine-tuned LLMs, properties that are considered confidential. The authors build their benchmark on the ChatDoctor dataset and consider two fine-tuning paradigms: question-answering (Q&A) and chat-completion. They propose two tailored attacks: a prompt-based generation attack and a shadow-model attack leveraging word frequency signals. Empirical evaluations across various pre-trained LLMs demonstrate the success of their attacks, highlighting a previously unrecognized vulnerability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper tackles a crucial and timely problem: the leakage of dataset-level properties from fine-tuned LLMs. While property inference has been studied in other model types (discriminative and generative), this work is one of the first to investigate it specifically in LLMs. The novelty lies in adapting property inference attacks to the unique characteristics of LLMs, considering their fine-tuning paradigms (Q&A, Chat-Completion) and the inherent complexity of text-based data. The proposed attacks, particularly the word-frequency based shadow-model attack, are novel approaches tailored to the LLM setting.

**Significance:** The findings of this paper are significant for several reasons:

*   **Practical Threat:** The identified vulnerability poses a tangible threat to data confidentiality in real-world deployments of LLMs. Organizations fine-tuning LLMs on proprietary datasets might unintentionally leak sensitive information, leading to breaches of confidentiality.
*   **Motivating Defense Mechanisms:** This paper underscores the urgent need for robust defense mechanisms to mitigate property inference attacks. The PropInfer benchmark provides a valuable standardized framework for future research and evaluation in this area.
*   **Filling a Research Gap:** The work fills a research gap by extending property inference studies to LLMs, which have distinct properties from other model types, such as discriminative or generative.
*   **Benchmarking:** The authors have provided a benchmark which offers a standardized way to measure property inference attacks on LLMs, allowing future works to compare performances.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-Defined Problem:** The paper clearly defines the problem of property inference in LLMs and highlights its practical relevance.
*   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** The PropInfer benchmark is well-designed, including different fine-tuning paradigms, diverse target properties, and realistic task configurations.
*   **Tailored Attacks:** The proposed attacks are specifically designed for LLMs, considering their unique properties and limitations.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The empirical evaluations are thorough, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed attacks across multiple pre-trained LLMs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Defenses:** While the paper acknowledges the need for defense mechanisms, it does not propose any concrete defenses beyond a brief mention of subsampling. Further research on effective defenses is necessary.
*   **Dependency on ChatGPT-4 for Labeling:** The reliance on ChatGPT-4 for labeling the gender property raises concerns about potential biases or inaccuracies in the labels. While the authors performed manual inspection, a more rigorous validation of the labels might be necessary.
*  **Data Contamination with Llama-3:** The authors admit that Llama-3 has most likely encountered the data they use for evaluation due to the release date. This makes their findings less credible.

**Overall Justification:**

While some limitations exist, the paper makes a valuable contribution by identifying and characterizing the vulnerability of LLMs to property inference attacks. The PropInfer benchmark and proposed attacks provide a solid foundation for future research in this area.
