The findings have practical implications for organizations deploying LLMs in sensitive domains and highlight the need for robust defense mechanisms. Considering the novelty and significance of this work, a score of 7 is justified.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### AutoGEEval++: A Multi-Level and Multi-Geospatial-Modality Automated Evaluation Framework for Large Language Models in Geospatial Code Generation on Google Earth Engine
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10365v1)
- **Authors**: Shuyang Hou, Zhangxiao Shen, Huayi Wu, Haoyue Jiao, Ziqi Liu, Lutong Xie, Chang Liu, Jianyuan Liang, Yaxian Qing, Xiaopu Zhang, Dehua Peng, Zhipeng Gui, Xuefeng Guan
- **Abstract**: Geospatial code generation is becoming a key frontier in integrating artificial intelligence with geo-scientific analysis, yet standardised automated evaluation tools for this task remain absent. This study presents AutoGEEval++, an enhanced framework building on AutoGEEval, and the first automated assessment system for large language models (LLMs) generating geospatial code on Google Earth Engine (GEE). It supports diverse data modalities and varying task complexities. Built on the GEE Python API, AutoGEEval++ features a benchmark dataset-AutoGEEval++-Bench-with 6,365 test cases across 26 data types and three task categories: unit, combo, and theme tests. It includes a submission programme and a judge module to realise an end-to-end automated evaluation pipeline from code generation to execution-based validation. The framework adopts multi-dimensional metrics-accuracy, resource usage, run-time efficiency, and error types-balancing hallucination control and efficiency, and enabling boundary testing and error pattern analysis. Using AutoGEEval++, we evaluate 24 state-of-the-art LLMs (as of June 2025), including general-purpose, reasoning-enhanced, code-centric, and geoscience-specific models. Results reveal clear performance, stability, and error differences across task types, model designs, and deployment settings, confirming AutoGEEval++'s practical value and scalability in vertical-domain code generation. This work establishes the first standardised evaluation protocol and foundational benchmark for GEE-based LLM code generation, providing a unified basis for performance comparison and a methodological framework for systematic, domain-specific code evaluation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AutoGEEval++: A Multi-Level and Multi-Geospatial-Modality Automated Evaluation Framework for Large Language Models in Geospatial Code Generation on Google Earth Engine":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AutoGEEval++, an enhanced automated evaluation framework for Large Language Models (LLMs) generating geospatial code for Google Earth Engine (GEE).  It builds upon a prior framework, AutoGEEval, by extending support for diverse geospatial data modalities and task complexities. The core components are the AutoGEEval++-Bench benchmark dataset (6,365 test cases across 26 geospatial data types and three task categories: unit test, combo test, and theme test), a Submission program (generates code based on prompts), and a Judge program (evaluates output accuracy, resource consumption, execution time, and error categories). The framework is used to systematically evaluate 24 state-of-the-art LLMs, and the results provide insights into model performance, stability, and error characteristics across different task types, model architectures, and deployment scenarios. The authors present the framework as the first standardized evaluation protocol for GEE-based LLM code generation and a significant advancement for geospatial AI research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel contribution to the emerging field of geospatial AI and code generation. While previous work has explored evaluating LLMs for code, AutoGEEval++ specifically targets the GEE environment, which is a significant and increasingly relevant platform. The expansion of the original AutoGEEval to incorporate diverse data modalities, multi-level task complexity (unit, combo, theme), and a more comprehensive benchmark dataset is a notable step forward. Furthermore, evaluating a larger set of LLMs is also a merit of this work.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in providing a standardized and automated evaluation framework for LLMs in the geospatial domain. This addresses a critical gap, as prior evaluations were either manual, limited in scope, or focused only on basic function calls. AutoGEEval++ enables researchers and practitioners to objectively compare the performance of different LLMs and identify their strengths and weaknesses in generating geospatial code. This could significantly accelerate progress in the field by guiding the development of more effective and reliable geospatial AI systems.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation Framework:** The framework offers a well-defined and structured approach to evaluating LLMs for geospatial code generation.
    *   **Large and Diverse Benchmark:** The AutoGEEval++-Bench dataset is a valuable resource for the community, covering a wide range of data types and task complexities.
    *   **Automated Evaluation Pipeline:** The Submission and Judge programs enable automated, end-to-end evaluation, ensuring reproducibility and scalability.
    *   **Detailed Performance Analysis:** The multi-dimensional metrics provide a holistic view of model performance, including accuracy, resource consumption, efficiency, and error characteristics.
    *   **Valuable Insights:** The evaluation results provide valuable insights into the performance of different LLMs in the geospatial domain, highlighting their strengths and weaknesses.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Focus on GEE:** While GEE is a popular platform, the framework's specific focus might limit its generalizability to other geospatial computing environments. It is not apparent if components of the framework, such as the Judge program, would readily transfer across other systems.
    *   **Limited Evaluation of Advanced Techniques:** The paper primarily evaluates the "native performance" of base LLMs and the authors acknowledged the exclusion of prompt engineering and RAG enhancements that may increase performance.
    *   **Dependency on External APIs:** The framework relies on the GEE Python API, which could introduce potential issues related to API stability and changes in the future.
    *   **Hallucination characterization:** The framework characterizes hallucination, but it is more like "hallucination under constraints" and there is little discussion of more creative "hallucination" like responses the LLMs might make when allowed greater freedom.

*   **Potential Impact:**  AutoGEEval++ has the potential to significantly impact the field of geospatial AI by providing a standardized benchmark for LLM evaluation. It will enable researchers to develop more effective and reliable geospatial AI systems and accelerate the adoption of LLMs in various geospatial applications.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The work makes a substantial contribution to the geospatial AI community by providing a much-needed evaluation framework. The comprehensiveness of the benchmark dataset, the automation of the evaluation pipeline, and the valuable insights into model performance justify a high score. However, the focus on GEE, the exclusion of other methods of performance improvement, and the limited focus on characterizing more unrestrained "hallucinations" prevent it from reaching a perfect score.

**Score: 8.5**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Revisiting Transformers with Insights from Image Filtering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10371v1)
- **Authors**: Laziz U. Abdullaev, Maksim Tkachenko, Tan M. Nguyen
- **Abstract**: The self-attention mechanism, a cornerstone of Transformer-based state-of-the-art deep learning architectures, is largely heuristic-driven and fundamentally challenging to interpret. Establishing a robust theoretical foundation to explain its remarkable success and limitations has therefore become an increasingly prominent focus in recent research. Some notable directions have explored understanding self-attention through the lens of image denoising and nonparametric regression. While promising, existing frameworks still lack a deeper mechanistic interpretation of various architectural components that enhance self-attention, both in its original formulation and subsequent variants. In this work, we aim to advance this understanding by developing a unifying image processing framework, capable of explaining not only the self-attention computation itself but also the role of components such as positional encoding and residual connections, including numerous later variants. We also pinpoint potential distinctions between the two concepts building upon our framework, and make effort to close this gap. We introduce two independent architectural modifications within transformers. While our primary objective is interpretability, we empirically observe that image processing-inspired modifications can also lead to notably improved accuracy and robustness against data contamination and adversaries across language and vision tasks as well as better long sequence understanding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Revisiting Transformers with Insights from Image Filtering" aims to provide a more interpretable and theoretically grounded understanding of Transformers, particularly the self-attention mechanism, by drawing analogies to classical image filtering techniques. The authors establish connections between self-attention, positional encoding, and residual connections with image processing concepts like bilateral filtering and boosting. They propose modifications to Transformer architecture inspired by these analogies, specifically introducing a "Bilateral Self-Attention" (BSA) and exploring alternative residual connections (Generalized Residual Connection - GRC). They claim these modifications can improve accuracy, robustness against data contamination and adversarial attacks, and better long-sequence understanding, supported by empirical results on several datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of linking Transformers to image filtering isn't entirely new; prior works have explored connections to denoising and nonparametric regression. However, this paper adds novelty by:

1.  **A more mechanistic interpretation:** The paper attempts to provide a finer-grained interpretation of specific Transformer components (positional encoding and residual connections) within the image filtering framework. This goes beyond simply saying "self-attention is like denoising" and tries to explain the role of individual components.
2.  **Bilateral Self-Attention (BSA):**  Removing the cross terms introduces a novel variant for the self-attention mechanism
3.  **Generalized Residual Connections (GRC):** Proposing a novel approach to residual connections that allows for memory of past information

**Significance:** The potential significance of this work lies in:

1.  **Increased Interpretability:** By providing a theoretical framework based on familiar image processing concepts, the paper could make Transformers more accessible and understandable to a broader audience, potentially leading to more informed design choices.
2.  **Potential for Architectural Improvements:**  The proposed modifications, BSA and GRC, while not groundbreaking, offer alternative design options that could lead to performance gains, especially in specific scenarios like long-sequence modeling or robustness against adversarial attacks.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Theoretical Foundation:** The paper presents a relatively rigorous theoretical analysis, drawing connections between Transformer components and image filtering concepts.
*   **Empirical Validation:**  The paper includes experimental results on multiple datasets (WikiText-103, LRA, ImageNet-1K, ADE20K) to support its claims.
*   **Addresses Limitations of Existing Frameworks:** The paper identifies shortcomings in previous attempts to understand Transformers through image processing and offers a more comprehensive framework.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Improvements:** The empirical results, while generally positive, don't demonstrate dramatic improvements over existing methods. The gains are often marginal and task-dependent.
*   **Limited Scope:**  The paper focuses primarily on the self-attention mechanism and related components. It doesn't fully address the role of other important aspects of Transformers, such as feedforward layers (even though the authors acknowledge it).
*   **Simplified Model:**  The theoretical analysis focuses on a simplified Transformer architecture (without MLP blocks), which may not fully capture the complexity of real-world models.
*   **Lack of depth:** Although the authors suggest interesting future directions for exploiting the connection with image processing (LARK kernels, fast bilateral filter approximations, etc.), they only offer conceptual ideas and fail to empirically investigate these concepts.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a valuable and well-reasoned analysis of Transformers through the lens of image filtering, the empirical gains are not substantial enough to warrant a high score. The theoretical framework, while sound, is not a complete re-thinking of Transformers. The proposed architectural modifications, while novel, offer incremental improvements rather than disruptive innovations. Also, the scope of the paper and the lack of exploration of more advanced image processing is a drawback.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### MLLM-Based UI2Code Automation Guided by UI Layout Information
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10376v1)
- **Authors**: Fan Wu, Cuiyun Gao, Shuqing Li, Xin-Cheng Wen, Qing Liao
- **Abstract**: Converting user interfaces into code (UI2Code) is a crucial step in website development, which is time-consuming and labor-intensive. The automation of UI2Code is essential to streamline this task, beneficial for improving the development efficiency. There exist deep learning-based methods for the task; however, they heavily rely on a large amount of labeled training data and struggle with generalizing to real-world, unseen web page designs. The advent of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) presents potential for alleviating the issue, but they are difficult to comprehend the complex layouts in UIs and generate the accurate code with layout preserved. To address these issues, we propose LayoutCoder, a novel MLLM-based framework generating UI code from real-world webpage images, which includes three key modules: (1) Element Relation Construction, which aims at capturing UI layout by identifying and grouping components with similar structures; (2) UI Layout Parsing, which aims at generating UI layout trees for guiding the subsequent code generation process; and (3) Layout-Guided Code Fusion, which aims at producing the accurate code with layout preserved. For evaluation, we build a new benchmark dataset which involves 350 real-world websites named Snap2Code, divided into seen and unseen parts for mitigating the data leakage issue, besides the popular dataset Design2Code. Extensive evaluation shows the superior performance of LayoutCoder over the state-of-the-art approaches. Compared with the best-performing baseline, LayoutCoder improves 10.14% in the BLEU score and 3.95% in the CLIP score on average across all datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LayoutCoder, a novel MLLM-based framework for automating the conversion of real-world webpage images into UI code. Addressing limitations of existing deep learning methods that struggle with complex layouts and rely on extensive labeled data, LayoutCoder comprises three key modules: Element Relation Construction (grouping similar structural components), UI Layout Parsing (generating UI layout trees), and Layout-Guided Code Fusion (producing accurate code with layout preservation). The paper also introduces a new benchmark dataset, Snap2Code, derived from real-world websites to mitigate data leakage issues. Experimental results demonstrate LayoutCoder's superior performance over state-of-the-art approaches based on BLEU and CLIP scores, particularly for websites with varying layout complexity. A human evaluation further validates the advantages of LayoutCoder in replicating layout, style, content consistency, and code usability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach by integrating three specific modules to address the challenges of UI2Code generation with MLLMs. The individual components (Element Relation Construction, Layout Parsing) are not entirely new concepts in the general sense (e.g., Scene Graph Generation, existing UI grouping techniques), but their combination and specific implementation within the MLLM framework to guide code generation for *web pages* does offer a novel contribution. The introduction of the Snap2Code dataset is also a positive contribution to the field, addressing the critical need for datasets that are less prone to data leakage.
*   **Significance:** Automating UI2Code has significant practical implications for web development efficiency and reducing labor costs. The LayoutCoder framework demonstrates a tangible improvement over existing methods, especially in handling complex layouts, which is a common bottleneck in current approaches. The robust performance across real-world datasets further emphasizes its practical significance.  The paper tackles the challenge of not just generating code, but generating *accurate* code that preserves the original layout's integrity. This is often neglected in other approaches which may prioritize functionality over visual accuracy.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** LayoutCoder presents a well-structured and integrated approach to the UI2Code problem. The modular design allows for a focused treatment of different aspects like layout parsing and code generation.
    *   **Practical Focus:** By targeting real-world webpage images and addressing data leakage issues, the paper demonstrates a clear emphasis on practical applicability.
    *   **Strong Empirical Validation:** The extensive experimental results, using both automated metrics and human evaluation, provide strong evidence for LayoutCoder's effectiveness and superiority over baselines. Ablation studies also provide valuable insight into the contribution of each module. The analysis based on webpage complexity metrics (DOM depth, tag count, etc.) is particularly useful in demonstrating the framework's scalability and robustness.
    *   **Addressing Key Limitations:** The paper clearly identifies and attempts to address the limitations of existing MLLM-based UI2Code solutions (layout comprehension, code accuracy).
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Component Novelty Detail:** As mentioned earlier, while the framework is novel, the individual components build upon existing techniques. A deeper dive into how the specific implementations of Element Relation Construction or UI Layout Parsing differ significantly from existing scene graph or UI grouping methods would strengthen the paper. It would be helpful to know what design choices made them particularly suitable for guiding code generation in this context.
    *   **Dataset limitations:** Snap2Code is a good addition, but it only includes 100 "unseen" pages. Having more unseen data would allow more of a solid determination of the systems capabilities. The evaluation of the "unseen" dataset only results in a handful of percentage point differences. It may be that even an extra 100 is insufficient to truly measure generalizability.
    *   **Generalizability Metrics:** The evaluation emphasizes BLEU and CLIP scores, both of which have known limitations. While the human evaluation partially addresses this, further discussion of the potential biases of these metrics and how they might influence the results would improve the rigor of the paper. Also, a deeper analysis in the types of errors or code quality issues that LayoutCoder overcomes would be insightful.

**Justification for Score:**

Based on the above evaluation, I assign a score of 7. The paper presents a novel and well-validated MLLM-based framework for UI2Code automation that demonstrates significant improvements over existing approaches. Its practical focus, comprehensive evaluation, and modular design are clear strengths. The main drawbacks are the incremental (rather than revolutionary) novelty of some components, Dataset size/validity for Generalizability tests, and a reliance on standard metrics without fully addressing their potential biases. While it doesn't entirely revolutionize the field, LayoutCoder represents a substantial step forward and provides a valuable contribution to the UI2Code research area.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Chance and Mass Interpretations of Probabilities in Markov Decision Processes (Extended Version)
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10377v1)
- **Authors**: Yun Chen Tsai, Kittiphon Phalakarn, S. Akshay, Ichiro Hasuo
- **Abstract**: Markov decision processes (MDPs) are a popular model for decision-making in the presence of uncertainty. The conventional view of MDPs in verification treats them as state transformers with probabilities defined over sequences of states and with schedulers making random choices. An alternative view, especially well-suited for modeling dynamical systems, defines MDPs as distribution transformers with schedulers distributing probability masses. Our main contribution is a unified semantical framework that accommodates these two views and two new ones. These four semantics of MDPs arise naturally through identifying different sources of randomness in an MDP (namely schedulers, configurations, and transitions) and providing different ways of interpreting these probabilities (called the chance and mass interpretations). These semantics are systematically unified through a mathematical construct called chance-mass (CM) classifier. As another main contribution, we study a reachability problem in each of the two new semantics, demonstrating their hardness and providing two algorithms for solving them.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a unified semantic framework for Markov Decision Processes (MDPs) that accommodates different interpretations of probabilities. It identifies two sources of randomness in MDPs: schedulers and transitions. It then proposes two ways of interpreting these probabilities: a "chance" interpretation (conventional probabilistic view) and a "mass" interpretation (viewing probabilities as rates of splitting mass/particles). By combining these interpretations, the paper defines four distinct MDP semantics. The paper provides a mathematical construct, the "chance-mass (CM) classifier," to systematically unify these semantics. Furthermore, it investigates the reachability problem under two newly introduced semantics, demonstrates the hardness of the problem, and proposes sound (but potentially incomplete) algorithms for solving them. The authors provide preliminary experimental results to demonstrate the practical applicability of these algorithms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the systematic unification of different MDP semantics through the CM classifier. While the individual "chance" and "mass" interpretations aren't entirely new, the explicit identification, categorization, and unification within a single framework are a clear contribution. The introduction of the two new semantics and the analysis of the reachability problem within those contexts is also a novel aspect. The complexity results and algorithmic approaches for the new semantics add further to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant issue: the proliferation of MDP semantics arising from different application domains (e.g., verification vs. dynamical systems). By providing a unifying framework, the paper facilitates a deeper understanding of the relationships between these semantics. This could potentially lead to the transfer of techniques and insights across different application areas. The study of reachability problems, particularly from a theoretical standpoint (hardness results), contributes to our fundamental knowledge of these models. The provision of algorithms, though incomplete, is a useful step towards practical application. The concrete examples Casino and Exam add insight to the new semantics.
*   **Weaknesses:** While the theoretical framework is well-defined, the paper's practical impact is somewhat limited by the incompleteness of the proposed algorithms and the small scale of the experiments. The undecidability and #P-hardness results are somewhat discouraging for direct practical applications in the new semantics, although approximation algorithms are still relevant. Furthermore, the use of global actions as in Remark 4. might obscure some potential applications with local actions.

*   **Justification:** The strengths of the paper are its systematic approach, well-defined framework, and explicit hardness results. The weaknesses are the incompleteness of the proposed algorithm, the scale of the experimental results and the possible restrictiveness of the actions. It is not trivial to link the proposed semantics with existing work. Finally, other works also exist to show how to mix the chance and the mass of probabilities, so this paper is not the first to touch on this specific issue.

**Score: 7**

The paper makes a significant contribution by providing a valuable unified semantic framework for MDPs, and its analysis of new semantic variations of the problem is not trivial.

- **Classification**: cs.FL
- **Score**: 7/10

### ReconMOST: Multi-Layer Sea Temperature Reconstruction with Observations-Guided Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10391v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanyi Song, Pumeng Lyu, Ben Fei, Fenghua Ling, Wanli Ouyang, Lei Bai
- **Abstract**: Accurate reconstruction of ocean is essential for reflecting global climate dynamics and supporting marine meteorological research. Conventional methods face challenges due to sparse data, algorithmic complexity, and high computational costs, while increasing usage of machine learning (ML) method remains limited to reconstruction problems at the sea surface and local regions, struggling with issues like cloud occlusion. To address these limitations, this paper proposes ReconMOST, a data-driven guided diffusion model framework for multi-layer sea temperature reconstruction. Specifically, we first pre-train an unconditional diffusion model using a large collection of historical numerical simulation data, enabling the model to attain physically consistent distribution patterns of ocean temperature fields. During the generation phase, sparse yet high-accuracy in-situ observational data are utilized as guidance points for the reverse diffusion process, generating accurate reconstruction results. Importantly, in regions lacking direct observational data, the physically consistent spatial distribution patterns learned during pre-training enable implicitly guided and physically plausible reconstructions. Our method extends ML-based SST reconstruction to a global, multi-layer setting, handling over 92.5% missing data while maintaining reconstruction accuracy, spatial resolution, and superior generalization capability. We pre-train our model on CMIP6 numerical simulation data and conduct guided reconstruction experiments on CMIP6 and EN4 analysis data. The results of mean squared error (MSE) values achieve 0.049 on guidance, 0.680 on reconstruction, and 0.633 on total, respectively, demonstrating the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed framework. Our source code is available at https://github.com/norsheep/ReconMOST.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "RECONMOST: MULTI-LAYER SEA TEMPERATURE RECONSTRUCTION WITH OBSERVATIONS-GUIDED DIFFUSION":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ReconMOST, a novel framework for reconstructing global, multi-layered sea temperature fields using observations-guided diffusion models. It tackles the challenge of sparse and irregular ocean temperature data by pre-training an unconditional diffusion model on a large dataset of historical numerical simulations (CMIP6). This pre-training allows the model to learn physically consistent spatial patterns of ocean temperatures. Then, during reconstruction, sparse *in-situ* observational data (e.g., from Argo floats) guides the reverse diffusion process to generate accurate temperature fields. A key advantage is that the pre-trained diffusion model implicitly guides reconstructions in regions lacking direct observational data, resulting in physically plausible results.  The authors demonstrate that ReconMOST can handle high levels of missing data (up to 92.5%) while maintaining reconstruction accuracy and generalization capability.  Experiments are conducted on CMIP6 and EN4 analysis data, showcasing the method's effectiveness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of diffusion models with sparse observational data for global, multi-layered ocean temperature reconstruction is a relatively novel approach. While diffusion models have been used in other climate-related tasks (e.g., cloud removal), their application to deep ocean temperature field reconstruction with extremely sparse data and the emphasis on maintaining physical consistency is a significant contribution. The "soft-extension" of observation-derived gradients to neighboring regions is a sensible approach to deal with sparsity, though its novelty is incremental. The authors have clearly demonstrated it helps the reconstruction.
*   **Significance:** Accurate ocean temperature reconstruction is crucial for understanding climate dynamics, global warming, and extreme weather patterns. The ability to reconstruct deep ocean temperatures from sparse data is a substantial step forward, as it addresses a major limitation of existing methods. This could have a significant impact on climate modeling and oceanographic research.  Extending ML-based reconstruction from surface (SST) to multi-layer/3D is also a positive step.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework effectively leverages both historical numerical simulations and observational data.
    *   The pre-training strategy allows the model to learn and enforce physical consistency.
    *   The method exhibits strong reconstruction accuracy even with extreme data sparsity.
    *   The paper includes thorough ablation studies to demonstrate the effectiveness of different components.
    *   The validation of the method on both CMIP6 and EN4 datasets, including evaluation on different simulation modes, shows good generalization ability.
    *   Clear explanation of the methodology and experimental setup.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on CMIP6 simulations, while practical, introduces a dependence on the accuracy and biases of those models. The potential impact of CMIP6 biases on the reconstructed temperature fields should be addressed more thoroughly. The reliance on pre-training data means the method is dependent on the availability of such data for any new area, but this is a characteristic inherent in many such ML approaches.
    *   The method primarily focuses on static 3D reconstructions, lacking the incorporation of temporal dynamics. While the authors mention future work on this, it's a notable limitation in the current framework, but they do touch on the matter when comparing reconstruction performance at different times.
    *   While the experiments cover different data modalities, the choice of hyperparameters (e.g., guidance strength, kernel size for soft-extension) seems somewhat ad-hoc. A more principled approach for tuning these parameters would strengthen the results. It is acknowledged that a functional relationship or an adaptive strategy for tuning these parameters could enhance reconstruction accuracy.
    *   The quantitative comparison with vanilla U-Net is weak and doesn't do the approach justice in arguing the case.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to advance oceanographic research and climate modeling by providing a more accurate and complete picture of ocean temperatures. It could contribute to improved climate predictions and a better understanding of ocean dynamics.

**Justification for Score:**

While ReconMOST is a valuable contribution, the limitations mentioned above prevent it from receiving a higher score. The reliance on CMIP6 models and the lack of temporal dynamics are significant drawbacks. Also, the hyperparameter tuning and quantitative comparison could be improved. That being said, the novel approach, the demonstrated ability to handle extreme data sparsity, and the potential for impact in climate science warrant a favorable assessment. The results are clearly better than existing reconstruction strategies.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Pisces: An Auto-regressive Foundation Model for Image Understanding and Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10395v1)
- **Authors**: Zhiyang Xu, Jiuhai Chen, Zhaojiang Lin, Xichen Pan, Lifu Huang, Tianyi Zhou, Madian Khabsa, Qifan Wang, Di Jin, Michihiro Yasunaga, Lili Yu, Xi Victoria Lin, Shaoliang Nie
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have enabled multimodal foundation models to tackle both image understanding and generation within a unified framework. Despite these gains, unified models often underperform compared to specialized models in either task. A key challenge in developing unified models lies in the inherent differences between the visual features needed for image understanding versus generation, as well as the distinct training processes required for each modality. In this work, we introduce Pisces, an auto-regressive multimodal foundation model that addresses this challenge through a novel decoupled visual encoding architecture and tailored training techniques optimized for multimodal generation. Combined with meticulous data curation, pretraining, and finetuning, Pisces achieves competitive performance in both image understanding and image generation. We evaluate Pisces on over 20 public benchmarks for image understanding, where it demonstrates strong performance across a wide range of tasks. Additionally, on GenEval, a widely adopted benchmark for image generation, Pisces exhibits robust generative capabilities. Our extensive analysis reveals the synergistic relationship between image understanding and generation, and the benefits of using separate visual encoders, advancing the field of unified multimodal models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Pisces: An Auto-regressive Foundation Model for Image Understanding and Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PISCES, an auto-regressive multimodal foundation model designed for both image understanding and generation.  The key innovation is a decoupled visual encoding architecture, which employs separate encoders and projection layers tailored for each task. This addresses the inherent differences in visual features and training processes required for understanding versus generating images. PISCES is trained in three stages: pretraining on image-caption pairs, fine-grained pretraining on detailed captions, and instruction tuning. The authors evaluate PISCES extensively on over 20 image understanding benchmarks and the GenEval image generation benchmark, demonstrating competitive performance in both areas. The paper also highlights the synergistic relationship between image understanding and generation, showing that training for both tasks within a unified framework can be mutually beneficial.  Finally, ablation studies validate the benefits of the decoupled visual encoders.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core contribution, the decoupled visual encoding architecture, represents a valuable advancement in the design of multimodal foundation models.  The idea of using different encoders and projection layers, and visual vector lengths for image understanding and generation is relatively novel. The three-stage training process, while building on existing techniques, is tailored well to the model architecture.

*   **Significance:** The paper presents compelling empirical evidence supporting the effectiveness of the decoupled architecture.  The results on a broad range of benchmarks demonstrate PISCES's strong performance in both image understanding and generation, showing it can compete with or surpass specialized models. The insights into the synergy between the two tasks and the ablation studies provide valuable information for researchers in this field. A strength is also the thoroughness of the experiments. The analysis of visual vector length and detailed caption pretraining contributes important nuances.

*   **Weaknesses:** While the decoupled architecture is promising, it's somewhat reliant on pre-trained components (LLaMA-3.1-Instruct 8B, SigLIP, etc.). The specific choice of these components may limit the model's generalizability or future improvements, even though it also allows for more efficient training. Further, while the benchmarks chosen are relevant, the performance comparisons could be improved by a more direct comparison with similar sized models that also employ separate encoder strategies if any existed. The computational cost of PISCES, while potentially lower than training a single model from scratch for both tasks, isn't extensively addressed, only relative. Also, the paper does not address the limitations of the current decoupled structure or its potential scalability challenges.

*   **Impact:**  The paper has the potential to significantly influence the design of future multimodal foundation models.  The decoupled architecture offers a practical approach to overcome the performance gap between unified and specialized models. The insights into the synergistic relationship between image understanding and generation could also encourage researchers to explore more integrated training strategies. The extensive benchmarking provides a valuable resource for evaluating future models.

*   **Rigor:** The experiments are well-designed and thorough, and the results are presented clearly. The ablation studies support the claims about the benefits of the decoupled architecture and specific training techniques.

*   **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow, although some sections are rather dense with technical details.

**Justification for Score:**

Overall, this paper provides a valuable contribution to the field of multimodal foundation models. While it relies on pre-trained components and there were few other decoupled approaches in the context of LLMs to directly compare to and the computational cost is not clearly defined, the novelty and significance of the decoupled architecture, combined with the strong experimental results and insightful analysis, justify a strong score. The paper directly addresses a key challenge in unifying image understanding and generation within a single model. The insight to explicitly account for the divergence between visual understanding and generative tasks through an architecture choice is a substantial contribution, and well supported through both the benchmarking and ablation studies.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Time To Impeach LLM-as-a-Judge: Programs are the Future of Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10403v1)
- **Authors**: Tzu-Heng Huang, Harit Vishwakarma, Frederic Sala
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are widely used to evaluate the quality of LLM generations and responses, but this leads to significant challenges: high API costs, uncertain reliability, inflexible pipelines, and inherent biases. To address these, we introduce PAJAMA (Program-As-a-Judge for Automated Model Assessment), a new alternative that uses LLMs to synthesize executable judging programs instead of directly scoring responses. These synthesized programs can be stored and run locally, costing orders of magnitude less while providing interpretable, and auditable judging logic that can be easily adapted. Program-based judges mitigate biases, improving judgment consistency by 15.83% and reducing biased responses by 23.7% on average compared to a Qwen2.5-14B-based LLM-as-a-judge. When program judgments are distilled into a model, PAJAMA outperforms LLM-as-a-judge on the challenging CHAT-HARD subset of RewardBench, outperforming metrics by 2.19% on Prometheus and 8.67% on the JudgeLM dataset, all at three orders of magnitude lower cost.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PAJAMA (Program-As-a-Judge for Automated Model Assessment), a novel approach to evaluating large language model (LLM) outputs. Instead of directly prompting an LLM to score responses, PAJAMA prompts the LLM to generate executable programs that encapsulate the judging logic. These synthesized programs can then be stored and executed locally, significantly reducing API costs while providing interpretable and auditable judging logic. PAJAMA addresses issues with high cost, uncertain reliability, inflexible pipelines, and inherent biases associated with standard LLM-as-a-judge setups. The synthesized program judges can be combined using weak supervision techniques to further improve reliability and reduce biases. Experimental results demonstrate that PAJAMA achieves competitive or superior performance compared to LLM-as-a-judge, particularly in bias mitigation and on challenging evaluation benchmarks, all at a fraction of the cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a compelling and potentially impactful idea. The core novelty lies in shifting from a black-box LLM-as-a-judge to a white-box, programmatic approach.

**Strengths:**

*   **Cost-Effectiveness:** The primary strength is the dramatic reduction in API costs. This is a significant barrier for many research groups and practitioners, making PAJAMA a valuable alternative.
*   **Interpretability and Auditability:** The program synthesis approach allows for inspection and modification of the judging logic, promoting transparency and enabling targeted interventions to reduce bias.
*   **Bias Mitigation:** The results indicate that PAJAMA can effectively reduce biases compared to standard LLM-as-a-judge methods, which is a crucial consideration for responsible AI development.
*   **Competitive Performance:** PAJAMA achieves competitive performance on established benchmarks like RewardBench, demonstrating its effectiveness as an evaluation tool. The outperformance on the CHAT-HARD subset is particularly noteworthy.
*   **Well-Defined Methodology:**  The paper clearly outlines the prompt engineering strategy for program synthesis, the criteria used to guide the LLM, and the weak supervision techniques used to combine judgments.
*   **Clear Writing & Structure:** The paper is well-written and organized, making the approach and results easy to understand.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on LLM for Program Synthesis:** While the idea of program synthesis is innovative, the reliance on an LLM (like GPT-4o) for program generation introduces a potential bottleneck. The quality of the synthesized programs directly depends on the capabilities of the underlying LLM and prompt engineering skills. There could be situations where the LLM struggles to produce effective judging programs for specific tasks or domains. This may limit the generalizability of the approach.
*   **Limited Scope of Judging Criteria:** The paper focuses on six specific criteria (Structure, Relevance, Readability, Bias, Factuality, Safety). It is conceivable that other important evaluation criteria might be relevant for different applications, and the process of expanding the framework to encompass these new criteria may require extensive experimentation.
*   **Evaluation Metric Focus:** The evaluation primarily centers on preference accuracy. While important, other metrics, such as correlation with human judgments or the ability to identify specific types of errors, could provide a more comprehensive assessment.
*   **Reproducibility Concerns:** While the paper describes the methodology, complete reproducibility might be hindered by the proprietary nature of the underlying LLMs used for program synthesis. Small changes to the LLM could influence the generated programs and, hence, the final results.
*   **Scalability Challenges:** While API costs are significantly reduced after the initial synthesis, generating a sufficient diversity of high-quality programs can still be challenging, impacting the overall performance and generalization.

**Significance:**

PAJAMA represents a significant departure from standard LLM evaluation methods. By encoding judging logic into executable programs, it offers a compelling path towards cheaper, more transparent, and less biased evaluation frameworks. The approach has the potential to influence various areas, including LLM alignment, agent verification, and dataset curation.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

PAJAMA is novel in its use of program synthesis for LLM evaluation, offering significant benefits in terms of cost, interpretability, and bias mitigation. However, the dependence on LLMs for program generation, the limited scope of evaluation criteria, and evaluation metric focus limit its immediate impact. Nevertheless, it represents a valuable contribution that could inspire further research and development in more scalable and transparent LLM evaluation frameworks. Improvements in program synthesis techniques, expansion of evaluation criteria, and adoption of broader evaluation metrics would substantially improve this approach.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### PAG: Multi-Turn Reinforced LLM Self-Correction with Policy as Generative Verifier
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10406v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhua Jiang, Yuwen Xiong, Yufeng Yuan, Chao Xin, Wenyuan Xu, Yu Yue, Qianchuan Zhao, Lin Yan
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities in complex reasoning tasks, yet they still struggle to reliably verify the correctness of their own outputs. Existing solutions to this verification challenge often depend on separate verifier models or require multi-stage self-correction training pipelines, which limit scalability. In this paper, we propose Policy as Generative Verifier (PAG), a simple and effective framework that empowers LLMs to self-correct by alternating between policy and verifier roles within a unified multi-turn reinforcement learning (RL) paradigm. Distinct from prior approaches that always generate a second attempt regardless of model confidence, PAG introduces a selective revision mechanism: the model revises its answer only when its own generative verification step detects an error. This verify-then-revise workflow not only alleviates model collapse but also jointly enhances both reasoning and verification abilities. Extensive experiments across diverse reasoning benchmarks highlight PAG's dual advancements: as a policy, it enhances direct generation and self-correction accuracy; as a verifier, its self-verification outperforms self-consistency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Policy as Generative Verifier" (PAG), a novel framework for improving the self-correction capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). PAG enables LLMs to alternate between the roles of policy (generating solutions) and generative verifier (evaluating the generated solutions) within a multi-turn reinforcement learning (RL) setting.  A key aspect of PAG is its selective revision mechanism, where the model only revises its answer if the generative verification step detects an error. This helps mitigate model collapse and enhances both reasoning and verification abilities. The authors demonstrate through experiments on mathematical reasoning benchmarks that PAG improves direct generation and self-correction accuracy compared to existing methods, and that the self-verification capabilities of PAG outperform self-consistency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The key novelty lies in the selective revision mechanism and the unified multi-turn RL paradigm. While previous work has explored multi-turn RL and generative verifiers, PAG distinguishes itself by integrating these concepts in a way that avoids the common problem of model collapse. The "verify-then-revise" approach is a conceptually simple but potentially impactful idea.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a core challenge in LLMs – the difficulty in reliably verifying the correctness of their own outputs. Improving self-correction is crucial for deploying LLMs in high-stakes domains. PAG's performance improvements on math reasoning benchmarks are significant.  The simplicity of the framework (no warm-up phase, no separate SFT) enhances its practical applicability.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined methodology.
    *   The selective revision mechanism directly addresses the issue of model collapse and unnecessary revisions.
    *   Strong empirical results on multiple datasets and using multiple models. The ablation studies further support the importance of each component.
    *   The demonstration that PAG-trained models can outperform self-consistency in self-verification is noteworthy.
    * The experiments included thorough comparisons, like comparing two-turn self-correction from PAG against three turns in Direct MultiTurn training.
    *   The write-up is very clear and well-organized.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on external ground-truth verifiers during training is a limitation. This restricts the framework's applicability to tasks where such supervision is readily available. The paper acknowledges this in the conclusion.
    *   While the authors experiment with different model scales, there may be diminishing returns in scaling PAG to much larger models without architectural modifications. This is not necessarily a weakness, but an open research question.
    * It would be valuable to see a better error analysis to know what sorts of problems PAG is better or worse at solving.

*   **Potential Influence:**  PAG has the potential to influence future research in self-correction for LLMs. The selective revision mechanism and unified RL framework could serve as a foundation for new approaches. The simplicity of PAG is a significant advantage, making it accessible to other researchers and practitioners.

* **Justification:** The paper presents a novel and effective approach to improve LLM self-correction. The experimental results demonstrate substantial performance gains on challenging reasoning tasks, highlighting the practicality and potential of PAG. The limitations, primarily the dependence on external ground-truth during training, provide clear directions for future research. Taking all these factors into consideration the paper earns a solid score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Reasoning RAG via System 1 or System 2: A Survey on Reasoning Agentic Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Industry Challenges
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10408v1)
- **Authors**: Jintao Liang, Gang Su, Huifeng Lin, You Wu, Rui Zhao, Ziyue Li
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) has emerged as a powerful framework to overcome the knowledge limitations of Large Language Models (LLMs) by integrating external retrieval with language generation. While early RAG systems based on static pipelines have shown effectiveness in well-structured tasks, they struggle in real-world scenarios requiring complex reasoning, dynamic retrieval, and multi-modal integration. To address these challenges, the field has shifted toward Reasoning Agentic RAG, a paradigm that embeds decision-making and adaptive tool use directly into the retrieval process. In this paper, we present a comprehensive review of Reasoning Agentic RAG methods, categorizing them into two primary systems: predefined reasoning, which follows fixed modular pipelines to boost reasoning, and agentic reasoning, where the model autonomously orchestrates tool interaction during inference. We analyze representative techniques under both paradigms, covering architectural design, reasoning strategies, and tool coordination. Finally, we discuss key research challenges and propose future directions to advance the flexibility, robustness, and applicability of reasoning agentic RAG systems. Our collection of the relevant research has been organized into a https://github.com/ByebyeMonica/Reasoning-Agentic-RAG.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a survey on Reasoning Agentic Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), a paradigm designed to overcome the limitations of traditional RAG systems when dealing with complex, real-world tasks.  It categorizes Reasoning Agentic RAG methods into two primary systems: predefined reasoning (following fixed modular pipelines) and agentic reasoning (where the model autonomously orchestrates tool interaction). The authors analyze representative techniques within each paradigm, covering architectural design, reasoning strategies, and tool coordination. Finally, the paper discusses key research challenges and proposes future directions to improve the flexibility, robustness, and applicability of these systems, framing the approaches through the cognitive science lens of System 1 and System 2 thinking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Timeliness and Relevance:** RAG and Agentic AI are rapidly developing areas. A comprehensive survey that synthesizes recent advances is valuable. The paper addresses a significant trend within the field.
    *   **Clear Categorization:** The distinction between "predefined reasoning" and "agentic reasoning" provides a useful framework for understanding the different approaches within Reasoning Agentic RAG. Connecting this categorization to System 1 and System 2 thinking provides additional clarity.
    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey appears to cover a wide range of relevant papers, including both academic research and industrial implementations.
    *   **Future Directions:** The discussion of research challenges and future directions is helpful in identifying areas that warrant further investigation.
    *   **Strong Writing Quality**: The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow. The introduction clearly lays out the problem and the scope of the survey.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Depth of Analysis:** While the paper provides a broad overview, the analysis of individual techniques could be deeper.  The descriptions sometimes feel superficial, lacking critical comparisons of the relative strengths and weaknesses of different methods beyond their general category. The lack of comprehensive evaluation in a tabular format is a significant missed opportunity to enhance clarity and provide direct comparisons of the discussed techniques.
    *   **Novelty of Synthesis:** The paper largely synthesizes existing work. While valuable, it doesn't present radically new insights or theoretical contributions. This is a survey paper; however, strong surveys often provide a more critical perspective that reshapes the understanding of the field.
    *   **Scope of Agentic Reasoning**: Focus is mainly on retrieval and integration of external data sources via agentic reasoning. The use of 'external tools' in the framework seems retrieval-centric, as agentic reasoning has potential to incorporate external APIs, databases for actions beyond just retrieving information.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper's significance lies in providing a structured overview of a rapidly evolving field. It can serve as a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners who are interested in learning about Reasoning Agentic RAG.
    *   The identification of key research challenges and future directions can help guide future research efforts.
    *   The paper's framing of the problem and the proposed solutions are well-articulated and contribute to a clearer understanding of the field.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, a score of **7** is appropriate. The paper offers a well-organized and timely survey of a crucial area in RAG research. It synthesizes existing research effectively and proposes valuable avenues for future work. The connection to System 1 and 2 is a welcome addition. However, the lack of deeper analysis and the absence of substantial original insights limit its overall impact. While it serves as a good overview, it does not significantly reshape the field or offer transformative perspectives. A few missed opportunities such as a tabular comparison and a lack of critical perspective hold the score down.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Burn After Reading: Do Multimodal Large Language Models Truly Capture Order of Events in Image Sequences?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10415v1)
- **Authors**: Yingjin Song, Yupei Du, Denis Paperno, Albert Gatt
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces the TempVS benchmark, which focuses on temporal grounding and reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in image sequences. TempVS consists of three main tests (i.e., event relation inference, sentence ordering and image ordering), each accompanied with a basic grounding test. TempVS requires MLLMs to rely on both visual and linguistic modalities to understand the temporal order of events. We evaluate 38 state-of-the-art MLLMs, demonstrating that models struggle to solve TempVS, with a substantial performance gap compared to human capabilities. We also provide fine-grained insights that suggest promising directions for future research. Our TempVS benchmark data and code are available at https://github.com/yjsong22/TempVS.
- **Summary**: The paper introduces TempVS, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the temporal grounding and reasoning abilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) using image sequences. TempVS consists of three main tasks: event relation inference, sentence ordering, and image ordering, each accompanied by a basic grounding test. The benchmark uses visual stories to create multi-event scenarios that challenge MLLMs to integrate both visual and linguistic modalities to understand the order of events. The authors evaluate 38 state-of-the-art MLLMs, demonstrating that they struggle with TempVS, showing a considerable performance gap compared to human capabilities. The paper also provides fine-grained analyses suggesting promising directions for future research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a critical and timely need for benchmarks that assess more complex reasoning capabilities in MLLMs beyond simple recognition or cross-image referencing. The shift towards temporal understanding in image sequences is a logical progression for the field, and TempVS fills a gap by focusing specifically on this aspect. The design of TempVS to avoid shortcuts that rely on single images, commonsense reasoning, or object identification from distractor options is a significant strength. The inclusion of multi-event scenarios is also a crucial contribution.

However, some aspects could be strengthened. The paper relies on multiple-choice questions, which, while facilitating structured evaluation, might limit the assessment of generative or open-ended temporal reasoning. While the evaluation of 38 models is extensive, the analysis focuses on overall performance and some specific linguistic structures. A deeper dive into the types of visual features or event complexities that pose the most significant challenges for MLLMs could provide more actionable insights. Furthermore, more detail could be added to describe the specific strategies used by the human annotators to solve the presented tasks and how to address it within the algorithm design space.

The paper's novelty lies in its specific focus on multi-event temporal understanding with rigorous controls to prevent exploitation of simple heuristics. It represents a meaningful step forward in evaluating MLLMs' capabilities for handling sequential visual data, making it a valuable contribution to the field. The identification of weaknesses in current models and suggestion of future directions adds to its significance.

Score: 7

Rationale: The paper introduces a valuable new benchmark with a clear focus and well-defined tasks. The extensive evaluation and initial analysis provide useful insights for the community. The design avoids several common pitfalls in existing benchmarks. However, the multiple-choice format and the potential for deeper, more fine-grained analysis could be avenues for improvement. Therefore, while the paper is a solid contribution, it isn't groundbreaking enough to warrant a higher score at this time. The contribution is novel, but the findings may warrant further consideration based on the experimental structure. The lack of detail describing the strategy implemented by humans to solve the task is also a weakness and should be discussed more thoroughly.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Sound Replace Vision in LLaVA With Token Substitution?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10416v1)
- **Authors**: Ali Vosoughi, Jing Bi, Pinxin Liu, Yunlong Tang, Chenliang Xu
- **Abstract**: While multimodal systems have achieved impressive advances, they typically rely on text-aligned representations rather than directly integrating audio and visual inputs. This reliance can limit the use of acoustic information in tasks requiring nuanced audio understanding. In response, SoundCLIP explores direct audio-visual integration within multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by substituting CLIP's visual tokens with audio representations and selecting sound-relevant patch tokens in models such as LLaVA. We investigate two configurations: (1) projecting audio features into CLIP's visual manifold via a multilayer perceptron trained with InfoNCE on paired audio-video segments, and (2) preserving raw audio embeddings with minimal dimensional adjustments. Experiments with five state-of-the-art audio encoders reveal a fundamental trade-off. While audio-to-video retrieval performance increases dramatically (up to 44 percentage points in Top-1 accuracy) when audio is projected into CLIP's space, text generation quality declines. Encoders pre-trained with text supervision (CLAP, Whisper, ImageBind) maintain stronger generative capabilities than those focused primarily on audiovisual alignment (Wav2CLIP, AudioCLIP), highlighting the value of language exposure for generation tasks. We introduce WhisperCLIP, an architecture that fuses intermediate representations from Whisper, as well as AudioVisual Event Evaluation (AVE-2), a dataset of 580,147 three-second audiovisual clips with fine-grained alignment annotations. Our findings challenge the assumption that stronger cross-modal alignment necessarily benefits all multimodal tasks; instead, a Pareto frontier emerges wherein optimal performance depends on balancing retrieval accuracy with text generation quality. Codes and datasets: https://github.com/ali-vosoughi/SoundCLIP.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Can Sound Replace Vision in LLaVA With Token Substitution?":

**Summary:**

The paper explores a method, called SoundCLIP, for directly integrating audio information into multimodal large language models (MLLMs), specifically LLaVA, without relying on text as an intermediary. The core idea is to substitute CLIP's visual tokens with audio representations, achieved by projecting audio embeddings into CLIP's visual space using a small MLP or by simply padding raw audio embeddings.  The authors discover a trade-off: tighter alignment with CLIP's visual space improves audio-to-video retrieval performance significantly (up to 44 percentage points), but it reduces text generation quality. They also introduce WhisperCLIP, a modified version of Whisper that fuses representations from different layers to better balance retrieval and generation.  Finally, they present a new dataset, AVE-2, containing 580K audiovisual clips with fine-grained alignment annotations for detailed analysis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its direct audio-visual integration approach, bypassing the common text-centric paradigm in MLLMs. The exploration of token substitution, specifically replacing CLIP visual tokens with audio embeddings is reasonably novel. The discovery and quantification of the retrieval-generation trade-off are also significant contributions. Further, WhisperCLIP’s layered information extraction method is a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The findings challenge the prevailing assumption that better cross-modal alignment *always* benefits downstream tasks. Demonstrating that stronger alignment can degrade generation quality is a critical insight. This trade-off suggests a need for task-specific optimization strategies in multimodal representation learning. The introduction of the WhisperCLIP architecture and the AVE-2 dataset provides valuable resources for future research. The emphasis on resource-efficient integration (a lightweight MLP) is practically relevant for deployment.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Rigorous experimentation: The paper comprehensively evaluates various audio encoders (ImageBind, Whisper, CLAP, AudioCLIP, and Wav2CLIP) across multiple datasets and tasks.
    *   Clear articulation of the trade-off: The retrieval-generation trade-off is clearly presented and well-supported by empirical evidence.
    *   Practical contributions: The introduction of WhisperCLIP and AVE-2 provides tangible tools for the research community.
    *   Strong quantitative results: The improvements in retrieval accuracy are impressive and the degradation in generation quality is well-documented.
    *   The analysis of Whisper layers and their relevance for retrieval is well done.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The token substitution approach, while effective, might not fully capture complex audio-visual interactions.  It essentially treats audio as another form of "vision" which can be a simplification.
    *   While the paper highlights the trade-off, it doesn't fully explore solutions for *mitigating* the generation quality loss. The proposed WhisperCLIP helps to some extent, but more work is needed.
    *   The improvement comes at the cost of significant degradation of the LLM's generation qualities and has limited the applicability of the token substitution technique as the optimal approach.

*   **Impact:** The paper can influence the design of future MLLMs by shifting focus from simply aligning modalities to carefully balancing alignment with modality-specific information preservation. The AVE-2 dataset will likely become a valuable benchmark for evaluating audio-visual alignment methods. The insights into language representation and the effect it has on generation are compelling.

*   **Justification for score:**
    The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by challenging a key assumption in cross-modal learning and revealing a fundamental trade-off. The empirical analysis is thorough, and the practical resources offered are beneficial. However, the solution to mitigate the generation quality degradation is not fully explored. While the work makes novel contributions to the multimodal realm, it does not present a groundbreaking advancement. It contributes to the understanding of the problem rather than providing definitive solutions.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.MM
- **Score**: 7/10

### Beyond the Battlefield: Framing Analysis of Media Coverage in Conflict Reporting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10421v1)
- **Authors**: Avneet Kaur, Arnav Arora
- **Abstract**: Framing used by news media, especially in times of conflict, can have substantial impact on readers' opinion, potentially aggravating the conflict itself. Current studies on the topic of conflict framing have limited insights due to their qualitative nature or only look at surface level generic frames without going deeper. In this work, we identify indicators of war and peace journalism, as outlined by prior work in conflict studies, in a corpus of news articles reporting on the Israel-Palestine war. For our analysis, we use computational approaches, using a combination of frame semantics and large language models to identify both communicative framing and its connection to linguistic framing. Our analysis reveals a higher focus on war based reporting rather than peace based. We also show substantial differences in reporting across the US, UK, and Middle Eastern news outlets in framing who the assailant and victims of the conflict are, surfacing biases within the media.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, focusing on rigorous assessment and a justified novelty score.

**Summary**

The paper addresses the important issue of framing bias in media coverage of the Israel-Palestine conflict.  It moves beyond simple qualitative assessments or superficial frame detection by operationalizing Galtung's framework of war vs. peace journalism using computational methods, including LLMs and semantic frame analysis. The authors analyze a corpus of news articles from US, UK, and Middle Eastern news sources, identifying differences in reporting across regions with respect to generic frames, issue-specific frames (based on Galtung's indicators), and semantic frames. Key findings include a higher overall focus on war-based reporting, regional variations in framing assailants and victims, and the identification of prevalent semantic frames associated with conflict. The paper makes the case that these framing differences can contribute to conflict escalation and polarization.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its *methodological approach* to operationalizing a well-established social science framework (Galtung's war vs peace journalism) using contemporary NLP techniques. While prior work has used NLP for frame analysis in general, *specifically* applying this approach with LLMs to Galtung's framework and *comparatively* analyzing the Israel-Palestine conflict across different geopolitical regions is a valuable contribution. It moves beyond simple "bias detection" towards a more nuanced analysis based on established conflict theory. The analysis goes beyond simple keyword analysis to incorporate semantic frame analysis which improves the robustness of the analysis.

*   **Significance:** The topic is highly significant. Biased media framing in conflict situations can have serious consequences.  The paper's findings regarding regional differences in framing, particularly concerning the portrayal of assailants and victims, are concerning and underscore the importance of media literacy and critical evaluation of news sources. The paper's operationalization of Galtung's framework provides a potential blueprint for future computational studies of media bias in other conflict zones. The exploration of differences across news organizations improves understanding of why there may be differences in reporting based on location.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Sound Theoretical Foundation:** The study is grounded in established theory on conflict reporting and framing.
    *   **Methodological Rigor:** The paper combines multiple NLP techniques (LLMs, semantic frame analysis, topic modeling) to provide a multi-faceted analysis.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** The comparison across different regions is crucial for identifying biases and understanding how geopolitical context influences reporting.
    *   **Clearly Defined Operationalization:** The paper does a good job of outlining how each indicator from Galtung's framework is translated into NLP tasks.
    *   **Qualitative Validation**: Acknowledging that NLP/LLM model-created outputs and labels can be erroneous, the paper claims to qualitatively validate the LLM results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **LLM Dependence:** The heavy reliance on LLMs introduces the risk of biases inherent in these models. Though the authors claim to qualitatively validate the LLM outputs, a more rigorous error analysis of the LLM is warranted. The relatively small dataset size used with LLMs also limits the generalizability of the conclusions.
    *   **Limited Generalization of the Corpus:** The conclusions are limited to the specific time frame (October 2023 - February 2024) of the collected data. Framing dynamics can change rapidly in conflict situations. The analysis of only English media is also a limitation, as media in other languages could have different biases and framing.
    *   **Interpretability of LLM Outputs:** Although prompted to output rationales for the classification, LLMs are still "black boxes". Interpreting the *reasons* an LLM assigns a specific label can be subjective and prone to confirmation bias.
    *   **Depth of Semantic Frame Analysis:** While semantic frame analysis is used, the paper could benefit from a deeper dive into the *specific lexical choices* within those frames that contribute to biased framing.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper can influence future research by providing a framework for computational analysis of conflict framing. It also serves as a valuable tool for media critics and educators to understand the complexities of media bias. The work highlights the need for greater awareness of the potential impact of media framing on public opinion and conflict dynamics.

**Overall Assessment and Score**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by computationally operationalizing a key social science framework for media bias analysis. While the reliance on LLMs and the relatively small corpus are limitations, the comparative approach and the focus on Galtung's indicators provide useful insights. The paper contributes to the important area of mitigating conflict by understanding media biases.

Score: 7/10

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### PAL: Probing Audio Encoders via LLMs -- A Study of Information Transfer from Audio Encoders to LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10423v1)
- **Authors**: Tony Alex, Wish Suharitdamrong, Sara Atito, Armin Mustafa, Philip J. B. Jackson, Imran Razzak, Muhammad Awais
- **Abstract**: The integration of audio perception capabilities into Large Language Models (LLMs) has enabled significant advances in Audio-LLMs. Although application-focused developments, particularly in curating training data for specific capabilities e.g., audio reasoning, have progressed rapidly, the underlying mechanisms that govern efficient transfer of rich semantic representations from audio encoders to LLMs remain under-explored. We conceptualize effective audio-LLM interaction as the LLM's ability to proficiently probe the audio encoder representations to satisfy textual queries. This paper presents a systematic investigation on how architectural design choices can affect that. Beginning with a standard Pengi/LLaVA-style audio-LLM architecture, we propose and evaluate several modifications guided by hypotheses derived from mechanistic interpretability studies and LLM operational principles. Our experiments demonstrate that: (1) delaying audio integration until the LLM's initial layers establish textual context that enhances its ability to probe the audio representations for relevant information; (2) the LLM can proficiently probe audio representations exclusively through LLM layer's attention submodule, without requiring propagation to its Feed-Forward Network (FFN) submodule; (3) an efficiently integrated ensemble of diverse audio encoders provides richer, complementary representations, thereby broadening the LLM's capacity to probe a wider spectrum of audio information. All hypotheses are evaluated using an identical three-stage training curriculum on a dataset of 5.6 million audio-text pairs, ensuring controlled comparisons. Our final architecture, which incorporates all proposed modifications, achieves relative improvements from 10\% to 60\% over the baseline, validating our approach to optimizing cross-modal information transfer in audio-LLMs. Project page: https://ta012.github.io/PAL/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "PAL: Probing Audio Encoders via LLMs - A Study of Information Transfer from Audio Encoders to LLMs" investigates how to improve the integration of audio information into Large Language Models (LLMs) for Audio-LLMs. It systematically explores architectural modifications to a Pengi/LLaVA-style baseline architecture based on hypotheses derived from mechanistic interpretability studies and LLM operational principles. The paper's key findings suggest that delaying audio integration until later LLM layers, processing audio information primarily through attention mechanisms, and using an ensemble of diverse audio encoders can significantly improve performance. These hypotheses are validated through a standardized three-stage training curriculum and dataset, demonstrating performance gains relative to the baseline. The resulting architecture, PAL, achieves relative improvements validating the approach to optimizing cross-modal information transfer in audio-LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Systematic Investigation:** The paper presents a well-structured and methodical exploration of different architectural choices for integrating audio into LLMs. This systematic approach allows for controlled comparisons and provides valuable insights into the effectiveness of various design decisions.
*   **Hypothesis-Driven Approach:** The modifications are driven by clear hypotheses grounded in mechanistic interpretability and LLM principles. This makes the study more than just a collection of experimental results; it provides explanations and rationale for the observed outcomes.
*   **Standardized Training Curriculum:** The use of a standardized training curriculum and dataset is a significant strength. It ensures fair comparisons between different architectures, which is crucial for drawing reliable conclusions about the impact of specific modifications.
