### A Crack in the Bark: Leveraging Public Knowledge to Remove Tree-Ring Watermarks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10502v1)
- **Authors**: Junhua Lin, Marc Juarez
- **Abstract**: We present a novel attack specifically designed against Tree-Ring, a watermarking technique for diffusion models known for its high imperceptibility and robustness against removal attacks. Unlike previous removal attacks, which rely on strong assumptions about attacker capabilities, our attack only requires access to the variational autoencoder that was used to train the target diffusion model, a component that is often publicly available. By leveraging this variational autoencoder, the attacker can approximate the model's intermediate latent space, enabling more effective surrogate-based attacks. Our evaluation shows that this approach leads to a dramatic reduction in the AUC of Tree-Ring detector's ROC and PR curves, decreasing from 0.993 to 0.153 and from 0.994 to 0.385, respectively, while maintaining high image quality. Notably, our attacks outperform existing methods that assume full access to the diffusion model. These findings highlight the risk of reusing public autoencoders to train diffusion models -- a threat not considered by current industry practices. Furthermore, the results suggest that the Tree-Ring detector's precision, a metric that has been overlooked by previous evaluations, falls short of the requirements for real-world deployment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel attack against Tree-Ring, a watermarking technique for diffusion models.  The attack leverages the publicly available Variational Autoencoder (VAE) used to train the diffusion model, a component that's often readily accessible. By using the VAE to approximate the model's intermediate latent space, the attacker can mount more effective surrogate-based attacks, significantly reducing the effectiveness of the Tree-Ring watermark detector. The authors show that their attack outperforms existing methods, even those that assume full access to the diffusion model. The paper also critiques the precision of the Tree-Ring detector, suggesting it's insufficient for real-world deployment scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in exploiting the publicly accessible VAE to attack latent-space watermarks. Previous attacks often made strong assumptions about the attacker's knowledge of the diffusion model itself. Reducing these assumptions by leveraging a readily available component (the VAE) is a significant step forward.  The critique of precision as a key metric is also a valuable contribution, as previous evaluations tended to focus solely on metrics like TPR and FPR.
* **Significance:** The paper's findings highlight a potential vulnerability in a widely used technique (latent-space watermarking) and raises serious questions about the current practice of reusing VAEs in training diffusion models. If the attack is easily replicable and generalizable to other latent-space watermarking schemes, it could have a substantial impact on the field. The argument that current evaluations of watermarking schemes overlook crucial metrics like precision offers a necessary corrective for future research.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem statement and motivation:** The paper effectively conveys the risks of AI-generated content and the need for robust watermarking.
*   **Well-defined attack methodology:** The attack is clearly explained, including the use of surrogate detectors and VAEs.
*   **Strong empirical evaluation:** The evaluation includes multiple datasets, baselines, and metrics, strengthening the claims.
*   **Practical relevance:** The attack relies on realistic assumptions about attacker capabilities (access to a VAE).
*   **Critique of existing evaluation practices:** The discussion of precision as a neglected metric is insightful and contributes to a more comprehensive understanding of watermarking performance.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **VAE dependency:** The attack's effectiveness hinges on the VAE being identical or sufficiently similar to the one used in the target diffusion model. While the authors argue that this is a realistic assumption, it's still a limiting factor. The ablation studies show a drop in performance when different VAEs are used. The effectiveness against customized/private VAE requires further work.
*   **Generalizability to other latent-space watermarking schemes:** While the paper argues that the vulnerability likely extends to other schemes, it doesn't provide direct empirical evidence to support this claim.
*   **Limited discussion of countermeasures:** While the paper suggests some potential countermeasures, it doesn't delve deeply into their effectiveness or feasibility. Further examination of advanced defense strategies would enhance the paper's practical impact.
*   **Open-source code dependence:** The attack relies on using an open-source implementation of PGD. There is a possibility that using other open-source attacks against tree-ring could lead to better outcomes.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could influence the design and evaluation of future watermarking schemes, pushing researchers to consider attacks that exploit publicly available components and to prioritize metrics like precision in their assessments. It could also lead to changes in industry practices related to VAE reuse and security.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by demonstrating a novel attack against latent-space watermarking using a realistic assumption (access to a VAE). It highlights a potential vulnerability in a promising technique and calls for a re-evaluation of existing security claims. The critique of evaluation practices and the focus on precision are also important contributions. However, the attack's dependence on the VAE and limited exploration of countermeasures slightly temper the score. The paper shows a strong technical contribution that can influence further research and real-world usage of watermarking.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Beyond Single-User Dialogue: Assessing Multi-User Dialogue State Tracking Capabilities of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10504v1)
- **Authors**: Sangmin Song, Juhwan Choi, JungMin Yun, YoungBin Kim
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable performance in zero-shot dialogue state tracking (DST), reducing the need for task-specific training. However, conventional DST benchmarks primarily focus on structured user-agent conversations, failing to capture the complexities of real-world multi-user interactions. In this study, we assess the robustness of LLMs in multi-user DST while minimizing dataset construction costs. Inspired by recent advances in LLM-based data annotation, we extend an existing DST dataset by generating utterances of a second user based on speech act theory. Our methodology systematically incorporates a second user's utterances into conversations, enabling a controlled evaluation of LLMs in multi-user settings. Experimental results reveal a significant performance drop compared to single-user DST, highlighting the limitations of current LLMs in extracting and tracking dialogue states amidst multiple speakers. Our findings emphasize the need for future research to enhance LLMs for multi-user DST scenarios, paving the way for more realistic and robust DST models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, including a score and a strong justification:

**Summary:**

The paper "Beyond Single-User Dialogue: Assessing Multi-User Dialogue State Tracking Capabilities of Large Language Models" investigates the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in Dialogue State Tracking (DST) when faced with multi-user conversations. The authors argue that existing DST benchmarks primarily focus on single-user scenarios, neglecting the complexities of real-world interactions. They extend an existing DST dataset (MultiWOZ 2.1) by generating utterances for a second user using speech act theory and LLM-based annotation.  They then evaluate several popular LLMs on both the original single-user dataset and the newly created multi-user dataset.  The results demonstrate a significant performance drop in multi-user DST, indicating that current LLMs struggle to extract and track dialogue states in the presence of multiple speakers. The paper highlights the need for future research to develop DST methods and prompt engineering strategies specifically optimized for multi-user settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its focus on multi-user DST, a relatively unexplored area within the broader DST research field. While there's a growing body of work on adapting LLMs for DST, the evaluation and analysis of performance with multiple users interacting within the same dialogue is novel. The specific method of generating the second user's utterances based on speech act theory and leveraging LLMs for this purpose adds further value. However, the idea of using LLMs for data augmentation is not new in general, but the application here is tailored and relevant.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the observation that current LLMs, despite their impressive performance in single-user DST, are demonstrably challenged by multi-user scenarios. This finding has practical implications for the development of more robust and realistic dialogue systems. It underscores the limitations of current LLMs and motivates future research directions.  The insights gleaned regarding error types (mis-extraction of slot values, incorrect slot type inference) further pinpoint the areas where improvements are most needed.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clearly defined problem:** The paper clearly identifies a gap in existing DST research and motivates the need for multi-user DST.
    *   **Well-defined methodology:** The approach for extending the dataset is systematic and based on established linguistic theory (speech act theory). Using LLMs for data generation is cost-effective.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The evaluation includes multiple LLMs of varying sizes and architectures, providing a broad perspective on the challenges.
    *   **Detailed error analysis:** The qualitative analysis of the errors contributes to a deeper understanding of the specific problems LLMs face in multi-user DST.
    *   **Open Source Code:** The research paper mentions that all code and data used for the research will be made open source, enabling reproduction of their results and advancement for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Realism:** The generated second user's utterances, while based on linguistic principles, might not fully capture the dynamics of real-world multi-user interactions. The interactions are somewhat artificial and constrained. More dynamic, negotiated dialogue structures could have been considered.
    *   **Dataset Size:** While extending MultiWOZ is a practical approach, the resulting multi-user dataset is still relatively limited compared to the resources dedicated to single-user DST. A larger and more diverse dataset could yield more robust and generalizable results.
    *   **Evaluation Focus:** The evaluation focuses primarily on zero-shot DST. While this highlights the inherent limitations of LLMs, it doesn't explore the potential for fine-tuning or few-shot learning specifically for multi-user scenarios.
    *   **Limited Mitigation Strategies Explored:** The study mainly highlights the problem and lacks exploration of specific solutions or mitigation strategies to improve performance in multi-user DST, even if only preliminarily.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in DST by raising awareness of the importance of multi-user scenarios. It will likely stimulate research on better prompt engineering techniques, new model architectures, or training strategies specifically designed to handle multiple interacting users.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper is a solid contribution. The method is well-designed and the execution is thorough. The findings, while not revolutionary, are valuable and point to a practical limitation of current models and therefore, open new avenues of future research in DST, making real-world conversational AI system possible.

**Score: 7**

This score reflects the paper's solid contribution to the field. It's not an exceptionally groundbreaking work, but it addresses a relevant and increasingly important problem (multi-user DST). The methodology is well-defined, the experiments are comprehensive, and the error analysis provides valuable insights. The limitations, primarily related to the realism of the generated data, prevent a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Edit360: 2D Image Edits to 3D Assets from Any Angle
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10507v1)
- **Authors**: Junchao Huang, Xinting Hu, Zhuotao Tian, Shaoshuai Shi, Li Jiang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in diffusion models have significantly improved image generation and editing, but extending these capabilities to 3D assets remains challenging, especially for fine-grained edits that require multi-view consistency. Existing methods typically restrict editing to predetermined viewing angles, severely limiting their flexibility and practical applications. We introduce Edit360, a tuning-free framework that extends 2D modifications to multi-view consistent 3D editing. Built upon video diffusion models, Edit360 enables user-specific editing from arbitrary viewpoints while ensuring structural coherence across all views. The framework selects anchor views for 2D modifications and propagates edits across the entire 360-degree range. To achieve this, Edit360 introduces a novel Anchor-View Editing Propagation mechanism, which effectively aligns and merges multi-view information within the latent and attention spaces of diffusion models. The resulting edited multi-view sequences facilitate the reconstruction of high-quality 3D assets, enabling customizable 3D content creation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Edit360 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Edit360, a novel tuning-free framework for editing 3D assets by propagating 2D image edits from arbitrary viewpoints while maintaining consistency across all views.  It leverages video diffusion models (V3DMs) and introduces an "Anchor-View Editing Propagation" mechanism, consisting of Spatial Progressive Fusion (SPF) and Cross-View Alignment (CVA).  SPF aligns and merges anchor-view and front-view trajectories.  CVA enforces structural coherence via cross-view attention. This allows users to edit a 3D asset from any desired perspective, resulting in high-quality, customizable 3D content.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the Anchor-View Editing Propagation mechanism. While V3DMs have been used for generating consistent multi-view images, the Edit360 framework allows edits from *arbitrary* viewpoints, a capability not present in previous methods which were typically constrained to the front view or a limited set of views.  The SPF and CVA components are well-motivated and seem to address the inherent challenges of inconsistent view fusion. The "tuning-free" aspect is also a significant advantage as it removes the need for retraining diffusion models.  However, parts of the method build upon previously existing V3DM architectures and well established diffusion model concepts such as using spatial attention to fuse view data. The combination of these elements to create a new editing pipeline is a valid innovation.
*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from the increased flexibility and user control in 3D asset editing.  Restricting edits to only front views significantly limits user creativity and workflow. By enabling editing from any viewpoint, Edit360 makes 3D asset customization more accessible and practical. The results showcase impressive consistency and detail, suggesting a practical tool.  The potential impact on areas like animation, gaming, and virtual reality could be substantial. Furthermore, the framework's ability to incorporate various input modalities (text, images, 3D models) is a strong point.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Flexibility:** Enables editing from arbitrary viewpoints.
    *   **Consistency:** Maintains geometric and visual coherence across views.
    *   **Ease of Use:** Tuning-free framework.
    *   **Versatility:** Supports various input modalities.
    *   **Strong Results:** Qualitative and quantitative results demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach. The visual results compare favorably to existing methods such as Tailor3D. The user study also indicates a preference towards Edit360.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on V3DMs:**  The framework's performance is inherently tied to the quality and capabilities of the underlying video diffusion models. Advances or limitations in V3DMs will directly affect Edit360. The quality of the V3DM also limits the editing precision.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While tuning-free, diffusion models are inherently computationally expensive.  The paper doesn't explicitly address the inference time, but it's likely to be a factor.
    *   **Limited Novelty with SPF and CVA**: While SPF and CVA enhance the approach, the core idea is leveraging the diffusion model architecture to accomplish the multi-view editing task. SPF and CVA, while important, might be seen as refinements.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in 3D asset creation and editing, especially in leveraging diffusion models for more interactive and user-friendly workflows. Future work could focus on improving the speed and efficiency of Edit360 or exploring other applications beyond 3D asset editing.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a good combination of technical novelty and practical significance. While the method builds on existing diffusion model architectures, it introduces a novel pipeline (particularly the "Anchor-View Editing Propagation") which tackles a significant challenge in 3D asset editing. The improvements in editing flexibility and view consistency are substantial. The weaknesses, such as the computational cost and reliance on V3DMs, are inherent limitations of the current state of diffusion models rather than fundamental flaws in the approach. For these reasons, a score of **7** is appropriate. While the advancement is notable, the method relies on pre-existing techniques with targeted refinements to produce the editing pipeline.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reliable Reasoning Path: Distilling Effective Guidance for LLM Reasoning with Knowledge Graphs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10508v1)
- **Authors**: Yilin Xiao, Chuang Zhou, Qinggang Zhang, Bo Li, Qing Li, Xiao Huang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) often struggle with knowledge-intensive tasks due to a lack of background knowledge and a tendency to hallucinate. To address these limitations, integrating knowledge graphs (KGs) with LLMs has been intensively studied. Existing KG-enhanced LLMs focus on supplementary factual knowledge, but still struggle with solving complex questions. We argue that refining the relationships among facts and organizing them into a logically consistent reasoning path is equally important as factual knowledge itself. Despite their potential, extracting reliable reasoning paths from KGs poses the following challenges: the complexity of graph structures and the existence of multiple generated paths, making it difficult to distinguish between useful and redundant ones. To tackle these challenges, we propose the RRP framework to mine the knowledge graph, which combines the semantic strengths of LLMs with structural information obtained through relation embedding and bidirectional distribution learning. Additionally, we introduce a rethinking module that evaluates and refines reasoning paths according to their significance. Experimental results on two public datasets show that RRP achieves state-of-the-art performance compared to existing baseline methods. Moreover, RRP can be easily integrated into various LLMs to enhance their reasoning abilities in a plug-and-play manner. By generating high-quality reasoning paths tailored to specific questions, RRP distills effective guidance for LLM reasoning.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper "Reliable Reasoning Path: Distilling Effective Guidance for LLM Reasoning with Knowledge Graphs" addresses the limitations of Large Language Models (LLMs) in knowledge-intensive tasks, particularly their tendency to hallucinate and their difficulty in reasoning over complex knowledge. The paper argues that simply retrieving factual knowledge from Knowledge Graphs (KGs) is insufficient; LLMs also need guidance in the form of organized, logically consistent reasoning paths. The authors propose a novel framework, RRP (Reliable Reasoning Path), to mine KGs for such reasoning paths. RRP combines the semantic strengths of LLMs with structural information extracted from the KG using relation embeddings and bidirectional distribution learning. A key component is a "rethinking module" that evaluates and refines reasoning paths based on their significance. Experimental results on public datasets (WebQSP and CWQ) demonstrate that RRP achieves state-of-the-art performance and can be easily integrated into various LLMs to enhance their reasoning abilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies in its holistic approach to KG-enhanced LLM reasoning. While existing works focus primarily on factual knowledge retrieval or chain-of-thought prompting, this paper uniquely emphasizes the importance of structuring knowledge into reliable and logically consistent *reasoning paths* *before* feeding it to the LLM. The "rethinking module" that evaluates and refines reasoning paths is a novel addition, addressing the problem of redundant or conflicting information that can confuse LLMs. The combination of LLM semantic capabilities with structured KG information via relation embeddings and bidirectional learning is also well-argued and effectively implemented.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a significant and pressing challenge in the application of LLMs to real-world tasks, where knowledge accuracy and logical reasoning are paramount. The experimental results demonstrate a substantial performance improvement compared to existing baselines, suggesting that the proposed RRP framework has the potential to significantly enhance the reliability and trustworthiness of LLM-based systems. The plug-and-play nature of the RRP framework, allowing for easy integration into different LLMs, further increases its practical value. The ablation studies offer valuable insights into the contributions of each component of the system. The robustness analysis also increases the significance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies a key limitation of current KG-enhanced LLMs.
*   **Novel Approach:** The RRP framework offers a unique and well-justified approach to address the problem.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation on multiple datasets, including ablation studies and robustness analysis.
*   **State-of-the-Art Results:**  The experiments demonstrate substantial performance improvements over strong baselines.
*   **Practical Value:** The plug-and-play nature of RRP makes it easy to adopt and integrate with existing LLMs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The Rethinking module has hyperparameters that have a significant impact on performance and require tuning, which could limit its usability in some scenarios. While the paper presents an analysis of these parameters, a more automated or adaptive tuning approach could further enhance its appeal.
*   **Scalability Challenges:** While the paper demonstrates strong results on the chosen datasets, the scalability of the approach to much larger and more complex KGs should be investigated in future research. The generation and evaluation of reasoning paths could become computationally expensive for very large graphs.
*   **Limited Case Study:** The case study illustrates the benefits of RRP, but is limited to one case only. More qualitative examples of the kinds of errors RRP prevents would be very helpful to enhance understanding.
*   **Dependency on External Knowledge:** The framework fundamentally relies on the quality and completeness of the underlying knowledge graph. In domains where KGs are incomplete or noisy, the benefits of RRP may be diminished.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of KG-enhanced LLM reasoning. The RRP framework offers a novel and effective approach to improve the reliability and trustworthiness of LLMs by focusing on structured reasoning paths. The experimental results are compelling, and the plug-and-play design increases its practical value. The weaknesses identified, while not negating the contributions, point to promising directions for future research. The paper has the potential to influence future research and development in this area.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**
The score reflects the paper's clear novelty in prioritizing structured reasoning paths, its strong empirical validation on knowledge-intensive question answering, and its potential to improve LLM reliability. The weaknesses, mainly pertaining to hyperparameter tuning, scalability, case study and dependency on KG quality prevent this from being an exceptional contribution (9-10). Nevertheless, the solid work in developing a functional and effective framework to address hallucination elevates it beyond a merely good paper (7), making it a very valuable addition to the research community.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### CogStream: Context-guided Streaming Video Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10516v1)
- **Authors**: Zicheng Zhao, Kangyu Wang, Shijie Li, Rui Qian, Weiyao Lin, Huabin Liu
- **Abstract**: Despite advancements in Video Large Language Models (Vid-LLMs) improving multimodal understanding, challenges persist in streaming video reasoning due to its reliance on contextual information. Existing paradigms feed all available historical contextual information into Vid-LLMs, resulting in a significant computational burden for visual data processing. Furthermore, the inclusion of irrelevant context distracts models from key details. This paper introduces a challenging task called Context-guided Streaming Video Reasoning (CogStream), which simulates real-world streaming video scenarios, requiring models to identify the most relevant historical contextual information to deduce answers for questions about the current stream. To support CogStream, we present a densely annotated dataset featuring extensive and hierarchical question-answer pairs, generated by a semi-automatic pipeline. Additionally, we present CogReasoner as a baseline model. It efficiently tackles this task by leveraging visual stream compression and historical dialogue retrieval. Extensive experiments prove the effectiveness of this method. Code will be released soon.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CogStream: Context-guided Streaming Video Question Answering":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the task of Context-guided Streaming Video Reasoning (CogStream), which addresses the challenges of video question answering in a real-time, continuous streaming scenario.  The task requires models to selectively attend to and reason from historical context to accurately answer questions about the current video stream. To facilitate research, the authors contribute a densely annotated dataset created via a semi-automatic pipeline, featuring hierarchical question-answer pairs.  They also present CogReasoner, a baseline model that uses visual stream compression and historical dialogue retrieval to efficiently tackle the CogStream task. Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of CogReasoner.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel and relevant task that more closely resembles real-world video understanding scenarios compared to traditional VQA benchmarks.  The emphasis on efficiently managing and selectively utilizing historical context is a significant departure from existing methods that often rely on brute-force processing of all available data. The proposed semi-automatic dataset generation pipeline contributes to the task. The novelty of baseline system lies in its architecture, rather than groundbreaking methods.

*   **Significance:** The CogStream task is significant because it highlights the computational and informational bottlenecks in current Vid-LLMs when applied to streaming video.  By emphasizing context-awareness and efficient processing, the paper pushes the field towards more practical and scalable solutions. A new dataset provides with good annotation density and a wide range of video genres is useful for this newly proposed task.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined problem:** The CogStream task is clearly articulated and motivated by real-world applications.
    *   **Comprehensive dataset:** The dataset addresses a key limitation in the field by providing densely annotated examples of context-dependent video reasoning.
    *   **Efficient baseline:** CogReasoner offers a practical approach to the task by leveraging visual compression and dialogue retrieval.
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of CogReasoner and highlight the limitations of existing Vid-LLMs on the CogStream task.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental contribution of the CogReasoner architecture:** The approach of CogReasoner is well motivated, but each component has been presented in other papers.
    *   **Lack of novelty of specific methods**: The visual stream compression techniques and dialogue retrieval approaches may not be entirely novel in themselves and are fairly simple (e.g. kmeans clustering). However, it is a systematic method for VQA task.
    *   **Limited generalization studies**: While the ablation study is comprehensive, the generalizability of CogReasoner on other datasets and with different underlying Vid-LLMs could be further explored.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in streaming video understanding, encouraging the development of more efficient and context-aware Vid-LLMs. The CogStream dataset will likely become a valuable resource for evaluating and comparing different approaches. The task definition opens the door for further exploration.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's novelty lies primarily in the introduction of the CogStream task and dataset, which directly address the challenges of streaming video reasoning. While the individual components of the proposed CogReasoner model are not necessarily groundbreaking, their integration within the CogStream framework demonstrates a practical and effective approach to the problem. The experimental results provide strong evidence for the importance of context-awareness and efficient processing in streaming video VQA. A wider contribution and more systematic experiments of CogReasoner architecture on different datasets could have improved the ranking.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Scientists' First Exam: Probing Cognitive Abilities of MLLM via Perception, Understanding, and Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10521v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhao Zhou, Yiheng Wang, Xuming He, Ruoyao Xiao, Zhiwei Li, Qiantai Feng, Zijie Guo, Yuejin Yang, Hao Wu, Wenxuan Huang, Jiaqi Wei, Dan Si, Xiuqi Yao, Jia Bu, Haiwen Huang, Tianfan Fu, Shixiang Tang, Ben Fei, Dongzhan Zhou, Fenghua Ling, Yan Lu, Siqi Sun, Chenhui Li, Guanjie Zheng, Jiancheng Lv, Wenlong Zhang, Lei Bai
- **Abstract**: Scientific discoveries increasingly rely on complex multimodal reasoning based on information-intensive scientific data and domain-specific expertise. Empowered by expert-level scientific benchmarks, scientific Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) hold the potential to significantly enhance this discovery process in realistic workflows. However, current scientific benchmarks mostly focus on evaluating the knowledge understanding capabilities of MLLMs, leading to an inadequate assessment of their perception and reasoning abilities. To address this gap, we present the Scientists' First Exam (SFE) benchmark, designed to evaluate the scientific cognitive capacities of MLLMs through three interconnected levels: scientific signal perception, scientific attribute understanding, scientific comparative reasoning. Specifically, SFE comprises 830 expert-verified VQA pairs across three question types, spanning 66 multimodal tasks across five high-value disciplines. Extensive experiments reveal that current state-of-the-art GPT-o3 and InternVL-3 achieve only 34.08% and 26.52% on SFE, highlighting significant room for MLLMs to improve in scientific realms. We hope the insights obtained in SFE will facilitate further developments in AI-enhanced scientific discoveries.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Scientists' First Exam: Probing Cognitive Abilities of MLLM via Perception, Understanding, and Reasoning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Scientists' First Exam (SFE), a new benchmark designed to evaluate the scientific cognitive capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs).  Unlike existing scientific benchmarks that primarily focus on knowledge understanding, SFE aims to assess perception, attribute understanding, and comparative reasoning skills. It comprises 830 expert-verified visual question-answering pairs across 66 tasks spanning five scientific disciplines (Astronomy, Chemistry, Earth, Life, and Materials Science).  The authors benchmarked 16 state-of-the-art MLLMs and found that their performance on SFE was significantly lower than on general knowledge benchmarks like MMLU and ScienceQA, highlighting a gap in scientific cognitive abilities. The authors conclude that SFE can facilitate further developments in AI-enhanced scientific discoveries.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel benchmark. The key contribution is the focus on *cognitive abilities beyond simple knowledge recall*. The categorization of scientific tasks into perception, understanding, and reasoning levels provides a more granular and nuanced evaluation. This taxonomy is a valuable framework for assessing MLLMs' scientific capabilities.  It moves the field away from simply testing knowledge base size and moves towards testing applied cognitive abilities relevant to researchers in these disciplines.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a clear and important gap in the evaluation of MLLMs in the scientific domain. Existing benchmarks are often biased towards knowledge understanding and do not adequately test the critical skills required for scientific discovery, such as interpreting complex scientific data, analyzing patterns, and drawing conclusions. By providing a challenging benchmark that assesses these cognitive skills, SFE has the potential to guide the development of more effective AI tools for scientific research. The coverage across multiple disciplines makes the benchmark useful across several scientific communities.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined cognitive levels:**  The three-level taxonomy (perception, understanding, reasoning) provides a structured approach to evaluating MLLM capabilities.
    *   **Expert-verified data:** The use of expert-verified VQA pairs ensures the scientific accuracy and relevance of the benchmark.  This is critical for ensuring the validity of the conclusions that are drawn from model performance.
    *   **Multidisciplinary coverage:** The inclusion of five scientific disciplines enhances the benchmark's generalizability and applicability.
    *   **Emphasis on native scientific data formats:**  Using raw data formats helps MLLMs in realistic tasks.
    *   **Comprehensive benchmarking:**  The evaluation of 16 models provides a robust baseline for future research. The bilingual nature expands the utility of the benchmark.
    *   **Openly available dataset:** Sharing the dataset contributes to the field and enables other researchers to build upon this work.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity of "Reasoning" Tasks:** The definition and assessment of "reasoning" could be further refined. It is challenging to differentiate 'understanding' from 'comparative reasoning' without further clarification on the required level of cognitive complexity. What constitutes high-order reasoning in a given scientific field is subjective and potentially benchmark dependent.
    *   **Evaluation metrics:**  While BERTScore and LLM-as-a-Judge offer certain advantages, they are imperfect. Using GPT-4 as a judge introduces bias. Future work could explore human evaluation or more tailored metrics for specific scientific tasks.
    *   **Limited "Generative" Assessment:** While the dataset is VQA-based, some of the most valuable scientific applications of LLMs involve generating hypotheses, designing experiments, or writing scientific papers. SFE does not directly evaluate these generative capabilities.
    *  **Potential dataset biases**: The choice of datasets used to construct the benchmark (native data formats) can induce unintended biases. Different formats induce diverse performance and it's important to acknowledge this point.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   SFE has the potential to significantly influence the development of scientific MLLMs by directing efforts toward improving perception and reasoning abilities.
    *   It can serve as a valuable tool for researchers in AI and scientific domains to evaluate and compare the performance of different MLLMs on scientific tasks.
    *   It can facilitate the development of new training techniques and architectures specifically designed to enhance scientific cognitive capabilities.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7.5**.

*Rationale:* The paper makes a valuable contribution by introducing SFE, a benchmark that addresses a significant gap in the evaluation of MLLMs for scientific discovery. The framework based on scientific signal perception, understanding, and reasoning allows for finer evaluation. The expert verification and comprehensive benchmarking are significant strengths. However, there are some weaknesses in the definition of the levels of ability and some aspects of the evaluation metrics (including the use of GPT as an evaluator) that prevent it from achieving a higher score. The benchmark's potential to influence the field by driving the development of more effective AI tools for scientific research is substantial. A few adjustments to the evaluation may be needed in future iterations.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### ALBERT: Advanced Localization and Bidirectional Encoder Representations from Transformers for Automotive Damage Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10524v1)
- **Authors**: Teerapong Panboonyuen
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces ALBERT, an instance segmentation model specifically designed for comprehensive car damage and part segmentation. Leveraging the power of Bidirectional Encoder Representations, ALBERT incorporates advanced localization mechanisms to accurately identify and differentiate between real and fake damages, as well as segment individual car parts. The model is trained on a large-scale, richly annotated automotive dataset that categorizes damage into 26 types, identifies 7 fake damage variants, and segments 61 distinct car parts. Our approach demonstrates strong performance in both segmentation accuracy and damage classification, paving the way for intelligent automotive inspection and assessment applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ALBERT, a transformer-based instance segmentation model for comprehensive car damage and part segmentation. It leverages Bidirectional Encoder Representations and advanced localization mechanisms to differentiate between real and fake damages, segment individual car parts, and classify damage types with high accuracy. The model is trained on a large-scale annotated dataset containing 26 real damage types, 7 fake damage types, and 61 vehicle part categories. The authors present experimental results demonstrating significant improvements over previous versions in damage localization and classification, particularly in distinguishing subtle damage types and fake anomalies. The paper also discusses future directions, including compressing the model into a lightweight student network called SLICK for efficient real-time inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Relevance:**  The paper addresses a practical and important problem in the automotive industry with applications in insurance, fleet management, and autonomous driving. The need for accurate and fine-grained car damage analysis is well-established.
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:**  ALBERT tackles several challenges simultaneously, including differentiating real/fake damage, fine-grained classification across multiple parts, and handling complex damage types.
    *   **Large-Scale Dataset:** The authors curated a large and richly annotated dataset, which is crucial for training a complex model like ALBERT.
    *   **Strong Performance:**  The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements in damage classification and part segmentation accuracy compared to earlier versions, including achieving 100% confidence in dent detection.
    *   **Clear Architecture:**  The paper clearly describes the architecture of ALBERT and its components, including the transformer-based encoder, localization head, and multi-branch classifiers.
    *   **Future Directions:**  The paper outlines promising future research directions, such as developing a lightweight version of the model (SLICK) for real-time inference on edge devices and incorporating multimodal data.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the paper presents a complete system for car damage detection, the individual components are not entirely novel. Transformer architectures and instance segmentation techniques are well-established in computer vision. The novelty mainly lies in the specific application to car damage analysis and the integration of existing techniques. The adaptation of existing computer vision techniques towards damage assessment could be seen as derivative instead of groundbreaking.
    *   **Limited Comparative Analysis:** The paper primarily compares different versions of ALBERT. A more comprehensive comparison with state-of-the-art methods in instance segmentation or damage detection would strengthen the evaluation. The absence of comparisons with pre-existing state-of-the-art techniques is a key weakness, making the true performance of ALBERT in relation to prior work difficult to ascertain.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Insights:** The paper is primarily focused on empirical results and architectural design. It lacks deeper theoretical insights or analysis of the underlying principles of the proposed approach.
    *   **Domain Sensitivity:** Although mentioned as a limitation, the model's potential domain sensitivity (e.g., to non-standard vehicles) could significantly impact its real-world applicability. Quantifying this sensitivity would be valuable.
    *   **Limited details on pre-training:** Pre-training strategies are mentioned as a contributor to the advancements from the v3 models. Without an extensive description of the steps undertaken, the paper leaves the door open for reproducibility issues.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper demonstrates the potential of transformer-based models for addressing a complex real-world problem in the automotive industry.
    *   The large-scale annotated dataset could be valuable for future research in this area.
    *   The proposed ALBERT model could potentially improve the efficiency and accuracy of car damage assessment in insurance, fleet management, and other applications.
    *   The future direction of developing a lightweight version of the model for edge devices is particularly promising for real-time applications.
    *   The paper lacks sufficient comparative analysis to clearly establish how significantly the model outperforms previous works and could be positioned as a marginal adaptation of current architectures.

**Justification for the score:**

ALBERT represents a decent engineering contribution. While the architecture adapts existing transformer architectures and instance segmentation methods and adds some specific multi-task learning elements, it applies them to a very practical problem in the automotive domain. The authors create a dedicated dataset and showcase the advancements achieved with their method. However, the novelty is somewhat limited since the model is mostly built upon known concepts, and there's limited theoretical contributions. The lack of an extensive comparison with previous architectures, along with a lack of detail about the pre-training steps are all areas to improve upon.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### AdaptiveLLM: A Framework for Selecting Optimal Cost-Efficient LLM for Code-Generation Based on CoT Length
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10525v1)
- **Authors**: Junhang Cheng, Fang Liu, Chengru Wu, Li Zhang
- **Abstract**: While Large Language Models (LLMs) have significantly advanced code generation efficiency, they face inherent challenges in balancing performance and inference costs across diverse programming tasks. Dynamically selecting the optimal LLM based on task difficulty and resource constraints offers a promising approach to achieve an optimal balance between efficiency and performance. However, existing model selection methods are resource-intensive and often neglect cost efficiency. Moreover, these approaches rely on human-annotated difficulty labels that are frequently inaccessible in real-world settings and may not align with the LLM's own assessment of task difficulty. In this paper, we introduce AdaptiveLLM, a framework that dynamically selects optimal LLMs for a given coding task by automatically assessing task difficulty. Our framework first estimates task difficulty using Chain-of-Thought lengths generated by reasoning model, clusters these into three difficulty levels via k-means, and fine-tunes CodeBERT to embed difficulty-aware features. A trained XGBoost classifier then selects the best model for each problem, optimizing the performance-cost trade-off. Experimental results show that AdaptiveLLM achieves a 7.86% improvement in pass@1 score while reducing resource consumption by 88.9% compared to baseline method ComplexityNet. When compared to a single model, AdaptiveLLM demonstrates an approximately 15% accuracy improvement, while maintaining the same level of cost consumption. Apart from that, the difficulty assessment using CoT provides more reliable selection criteria than human evaluation. Our replication package is available at https://github.com/cjhCoder7/AdaptiveLLM.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AdaptiveLLM, a framework designed to dynamically select the most cost-efficient Large Language Model (LLM) for code generation tasks. The framework automates task difficulty assessment using the Chain-of-Thought (CoT) length generated by a reasoning model.  It clusters these CoT lengths into difficulty levels using k-means and fine-tunes CodeBERT to embed difficulty-aware features. An XGBoost classifier then selects the best model for each problem, balancing performance and cost.  Experiments demonstrate that AdaptiveLLM improves pass@1 scores while reducing resource consumption compared to baselines like ComplexityNet.  The paper argues that CoT provides a more reliable assessment of difficulty than human-annotated labels and open-sources their replication package.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in automating LLM selection for code generation by using CoT length as a proxy for task difficulty and embedding this into the selection process. While other works have explored LLM selection and difficulty estimation, AdaptiveLLM's approach of combining CoT length, k-means clustering, CodeBERT fine-tuning, and XGBoost classification offers a relatively new combination of techniques.  However, each of these individual components is well-established. The novelty rests in the integration.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in the LLM space: balancing performance with computational cost. Code generation, while powerful, is often expensive.  Dynamically selecting the right model can significantly reduce costs, particularly for projects with diverse coding tasks. The experimental results, showing improvements in pass@1 and substantial reductions in resource consumption, are encouraging. It highlights the importance of cost-effective methods in LLM applications.
* **Strengths:**
    * It integrates several established techniques (CoT, CodeBERT, XGBoost, k-means) into a novel framework.
    * Provides a cost-effective solution for LLM selection in code generation.
    * Claims to outperform human-annotated difficulty assessments with automated approach.
    * Offers a comprehensive evaluation with comparison to SOTA baseline, ComplexityNet.
    * Open-sources replication package.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * The framework relies on CoT length, which is not always a perfect indicator of task difficulty.  While the paper argues it's better than human labels, there's still randomness in LLM's CoT behavior.
    * The choice of k-means clustering for difficulty levels is somewhat arbitrary. Other clustering or classification methods could be explored.
    * While the experiments show promising results, the generalizability to other types of coding tasks or different LLM architectures is not fully established.  The specific datasets (HumanEval, LeetCode, CodeContests) used have different characteristics, but broadening the evaluation would strengthen the claims.
    * The study excludes instances where the CoT length exceeds the maximum token limit. Though rare, this pre-processing step still might lead to certain bias.
    * Construct validity: The cost model relies on SiliconFlow data or proxies. The variations of actual cloud cost across different services, region and time can impact results. This can make the claimed percentages less precise.
    * The contribution does not include any architectural innovation to the underlying code models themselves.

*   **Potential Influence:** If validated by further research, AdaptiveLLM could influence how LLMs are deployed for code generation, encouraging more cost-aware strategies. The open-source nature of the project facilitates further investigation and adaptation by other researchers and practitioners.

**Justification for Score:**

AdaptiveLLM presents a reasonably innovative solution to a significant problem in the LLM field.  It combines existing techniques in a novel way to achieve demonstrable cost savings and performance gains.  The automated difficulty assessment using CoT is a clever idea. However, the reliance on CoT and the use of existing LLMs as building blocks limit the overall transformative impact. The limitations mentioned above also hinder the score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### LogiPlan: A Structured Benchmark for Logical Planning and Relational Reasoning in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10527v1)
- **Authors**: Yanan Cai, Ahmed Salem, Besmira Nushi, Mark Russinovich
- **Abstract**: We introduce LogiPlan, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate the capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in logical planning and reasoning over complex relational structures. Logical relational reasoning is important for applications that may rely on LLMs to generate and query structured graphs of relations such as network infrastructure, knowledge bases, or business process schema. Our framework allows for dynamic variation of task complexity by controlling the number of objects, relations, and the minimum depth of relational chains, providing a fine-grained assessment of model performance across difficulty levels. LogiPlan encompasses three complementary tasks: (1) Plan Generation, where models must construct valid directed relational graphs meeting specified structural constraints; (2) Consistency Detection, testing models' ability to identify inconsistencies in relational structures; and (3) Comparison Question, evaluating models' capacity to determine the validity of queried relationships within a given graph. Additionally, we assess models' self-correction capabilities by prompting them to verify and refine their initial solutions. We evaluate state-of-the-art models including DeepSeek R1, Gemini 2.0 Pro, Gemini 2 Flash Thinking, GPT-4.5, GPT-4o, Llama 3.1 405B, O3-mini, O1, and Claude 3.7 Sonnet across these tasks, revealing significant performance gaps that correlate with model scale and architecture. Our analysis demonstrates that while recent reasoning-enhanced models show promising results on simpler instances, they struggle with more complex configurations requiring deeper logical planning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LogiPlan: A Structured Benchmark for Logical Planning and Relational Reasoning in LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LOGIPLAN, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate large language models (LLMs) on logical planning and relational reasoning tasks. It focuses on the ability of LLMs to generate and query structured graphs of relations, a capability crucial for applications like network infrastructure management, knowledge base querying, and business process schema validation.  LOGIPLAN consists of three tasks: Plan Generation (constructing relational graphs), Consistency Detection (identifying inconsistencies in graphs), and Comparison Question (evaluating the validity of relationships in graphs). The benchmark allows for dynamic variation of task complexity by controlling the number of objects, relations, and the minimum depth of relational chains. The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art LLMs, analyze performance gaps, and assess models' self-correction capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Benchmark:** The core strength is the introduction of a new benchmark specifically tailored for relational reasoning and planning. This is a valuable contribution as existing LLM benchmarks often lack a focused evaluation on these crucial capabilities.
    *   **Task Diversity:** LOGIPLAN's three tasks (Plan Generation, Consistency Detection, and Comparison Question) offer a comprehensive assessment of relational reasoning abilities from construction to verification and querying.
    *   **Controllable Complexity:** The benchmark enables fine-grained control over task complexity, allowing researchers to analyze model performance across a wide range of difficulty levels. This is superior to static benchmarks.
    *   **Focus on a Crucial Capability:** Relational reasoning is fundamental for many real-world applications of LLMs, making this benchmark highly relevant. The paper clearly articulates the importance of the targeted skills.
    *   **Self-Correction Evaluation:**  The inclusion of self-correction evaluation is a valuable addition, assessing not just the initial solution but also the LLM's ability to refine and improve its reasoning.
    *   **Extensive Evaluation:** The paper presents results from a thorough evaluation of nine state-of-the-art models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Data:** The benchmark relies entirely on synthetically generated data.  While this provides control, it might not fully capture the nuances and complexities of real-world relational data, potentially limiting the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Limited Real-World Scenarios:** The paper focuses on generic relational structures (A > B > C). While the motivation draws from application domains like supply chain and code analysis, the benchmarked tasks are somewhat abstract. Demonstrating how LOGIPLAN translates to specific, complex, real-world instances would strengthen the work.
    *   **Algorithmic Simplicity of Solutions:**  The Plan Generation task, while seemingly successful for some models, appears to be solved by models learning a very straightforward ordering algorithm (A > B > C > ...).  This suggests the task might be less challenging than intended, and the models are not necessarily exhibiting sophisticated *planning*.  The robustness of such models to diverse input structures is questionable.
    *   **Lack of Error Analysis:** The paper presents performance scores, but a more detailed qualitative error analysis would be beneficial. Understanding the specific types of errors that models make in each task (e.g., types of inconsistencies missed, common mistakes in comparison questions) would provide valuable insights for future research.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:** Although the benchmark scales the number of objects and relations, the *depth* of the reasoning required in each task (especially Consistency Detection) may still be limited. The paper could benefit from exploring truly deep reasoning scenarios (e.g., cycle detection with many interconnected steps).
    *   **Limited Zero-Shot Generalization Exploration:** The paper primarily analyzes performance *within* the LOGIPLAN framework. While the authors state it can be "generates fresh or private copies of the benchmark," exploration of transfer learning to new domains (e.g. supply chain) is missing, hurting practical relevance.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The paper's novelty primarily stems from the design and introduction of the LOGIPLAN benchmark itself. While the individual tasks (graph construction, consistency checking, relation querying) are not entirely new concepts in AI, their combination within a unified, controllable benchmark for LLMs is a distinct contribution. The significance lies in providing a dedicated tool for evaluating and improving the relational reasoning and planning abilities of LLMs, which are crucial for their application in real-world problems. However, the reliance on synthetic data and the potential for models to learn simple algorithmic solutions to some tasks somewhat diminish the potential impact.

**Justification for Score:**

The LOGIPLAN benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation by focusing on relational reasoning and planning. The controllable complexity, the inclusion of self-correction evaluation, and the comprehensive evaluation of several models are all valuable aspects of the work. However, the limitations of using synthetic data, the potential for models to exploit algorithmic shortcuts, and the limited exploration of complex, real-world scenarios prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. The absence of in-depth error analysis further limits the insights gained. Overall, I believe that the introduction of the benchmark represents a moderate advancement.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Equivariant Neural Diffusion for Molecule Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10532v1)
- **Authors**: François Cornet, Grigory Bartosh, Mikkel N. Schmidt, Christian A. Naesseth
- **Abstract**: We introduce Equivariant Neural Diffusion (END), a novel diffusion model for molecule generation in 3D that is equivariant to Euclidean transformations. Compared to current state-of-the-art equivariant diffusion models, the key innovation in END lies in its learnable forward process for enhanced generative modelling. Rather than pre-specified, the forward process is parameterized through a time- and data-dependent transformation that is equivariant to rigid transformations. Through a series of experiments on standard molecule generation benchmarks, we demonstrate the competitive performance of END compared to several strong baselines for both unconditional and conditional generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Equivariant Neural Diffusion (END), a novel diffusion model for 3D molecule generation.  The core innovation lies in using a *learnable* forward process, rather than a pre-specified one, to corrupt the data distribution. This learnable forward process is parameterized by a time- and data-dependent equivariant transformation. The authors demonstrate that END achieves competitive performance on standard molecule generation benchmarks (QM9 and GEOM-Drugs) compared to existing equivariant diffusion models, particularly in conditional generation scenarios, showing improved controllability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The key novelty is the learnable forward diffusion process in an equivariant setting. While learnable forward processes have been explored in general diffusion models, their application in 3D molecule generation with Euclidean equivariance is a significant contribution. This enables a more flexible and data-adaptive corruption process. Specifically, this is where most existing diffusion models for molecules pre-specify the forward process, restricting the flexibility of the latent space and challenge the training for reverse process. In essence, replacing fixed forward process by learnable counterparts, their END model showed improved results against traditional approaches.
*   **Significance:** The improved performance in conditional generation is particularly significant. The ability to control molecule generation based on composition and substructure constraints is crucial for practical applications like drug discovery. While existing equivariant diffusion models address the symmetry requirements of 3D molecules, END's learnable forward process enhances controllability and performance in targeted molecule generation.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides extensive experimental results on established benchmarks, demonstrating competitive or superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods. The ablation studies effectively highlight the benefits of the learnable forward process. The conditional generation results are particularly compelling.
    *   **Sound Theoretical Foundation:**  The authors rigorously establish the equivariance of their model and demonstrate that the reverse SDE remains equivariant with their learnable forward process. The mathematical framework is well-defined.
    *   **Clear Presentation:**  The paper is well-written and clearly explains the technical details of the model and the experimental setup. The figures and tables are informative.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** The increased computational cost during training and sampling is a significant drawback. While the paper acknowledges this and suggests potential optimizations (learning the score rather than the full forward process), the current implementation is slower than existing methods. Although END requires fewer steps to achieve better results, each step is slower as well.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The experiments are primarily focused on organic molecules and specific benchmark datasets. It is unclear whether the same benefits would be observed on other types of molecules or materials. Also, metrics, while widely used in the community, do have limitations and requires QM validations for full assessment.
    *   **Scalability concerns:** END's fully-connected graph approach for molecule representation means it suffers from scalability problems for larger molecules.
    *   **No detailed discussion about alternative forward models:** The paper explores a single learnable model forward process. It is not known whether other model designs may produce superior results than END, especially with careful consideration on reducing the added computational burden.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The idea of a learnable forward diffusion process could have a significant impact on the field of generative modeling for molecules and other 3D structures. This could lead to more controllable and efficient generative models.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper's novelty is significant and its performance improvements in conditional generation are promising, the increased computational cost is a real concern that needs to be addressed for practical applications. Also, while the performance improves and achieves competitive result, it does not significantly outperform other state-of-the-art diffusion methods such as GEOLDM or GEOBFN. Therefore, the paper is novel and significant, but further improvements are needed to make it truly impactful. The experimental range for evaluation is relatively narrow in that molecule structures with diverse ranges of features were not tested.

### StepProof: Step-by-step verification of natural language mathematical proofs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10558v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaolin Hu, Qinghua Zhou, Bogdan Grechuk, Ivan Y. Tyukin
- **Abstract**: Interactive theorem provers (ITPs) are powerful tools for the formal verification of mathematical proofs down to the axiom level. However, their lack of a natural language interface remains a significant limitation. Recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) have enhanced the understanding of natural language inputs, paving the way for autoformalization - the process of translating natural language proofs into formal proofs that can be verified. Despite these advancements, existing autoformalization approaches are limited to verifying complete proofs and lack the capability for finer, sentence-level verification. To address this gap, we propose StepProof, a novel autoformalization method designed for granular, step-by-step verification. StepProof breaks down complete proofs into multiple verifiable subproofs, enabling sentence-level verification. Experimental results demonstrate that StepProof significantly improves proof success rates and efficiency compared to traditional methods. Additionally, we found that minor manual adjustments to the natural language proofs, tailoring them for step-level verification, further enhanced StepProof's performance in autoformalization.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces StepProof, a novel autoformalization method for verifying mathematical proofs written in natural language. Unlike traditional "full-proof" approaches that attempt to formalize entire proofs at once, StepProof adopts a granular, step-by-step verification strategy. It decomposes proofs into verifiable subproofs, enabling sentence-level verification and providing more detailed feedback to users.  The paper presents experimental results on the GSM8K dataset, demonstrating that StepProof improves proof success rates, efficiency, and stability compared to existing methods. Furthermore, the authors investigate the impact of natural language proof writing style on the success of formalization and explore ways to improve the compatibility between informal and formal representations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The step-by-step approach to autoformalization is a valuable contribution. The decomposition of a complex task into smaller, manageable subtasks mirrors successful strategies in other areas of AI and program synthesis. StepProof's sentence-level granularity enables finer-grained control, debugging, and user interaction, addressing key limitations of existing full-proof methods. While the LEGO-Prover has similar idea, it requires some generation of the sub-proof formal statement generation, increasing error probability of formalization. StepProof is an enhancement and innovation over LEGO-Prover.

*   **Significance:** Autoformalization is a crucial bottleneck in making theorem provers accessible to a broader audience. By improving the efficiency and reliability of translating natural language proofs into formal proofs, this research brings formal verification closer to mainstream mathematical practice. The ability to pinpoint errors at the sentence level provides more actionable feedback to users, facilitating a more interactive and user-friendly verification process.
    The exploration of natural language proof styles and their impact on formalization is another valuable contribution. Understanding how informal language influences formalization success can inform the design of better translation algorithms and guidelines for writing more formalizable proofs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of full-proof autoformalization methods.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The proposed StepProof method is a well-motivated and innovative solution.
    *   **Empirical Validation:**  The experimental results on GSM8K provide compelling evidence of StepProof's superior performance. The experiments include comparisons against both full-proof strategies and existing automated formal proof methods.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper includes a thoughtful analysis of the factors influencing formalization success, such as natural language proof style and LLM limitations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Model Scale:** The experiments are conducted using a relatively small open-source language model (Llama3 8B-Instruct). While this is a commendable effort to evaluate on open-source models, it's unclear how well StepProof would scale to larger, more powerful models and more complex mathematical domains. The paper also acknowledges this limitation.
    *   **Dataset Limitation:** The GSM8K dataset, while suitable for evaluating quantitative reasoning, may not fully capture the nuances and complexities of more advanced mathematical proofs.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Structured Proofs:** The paper acknowledges StepProof's limitations in handling structured proofs, which are common in many mathematical domains. More research is needed to address this limitation.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   **Increased Accessibility of Theorem Provers:** StepProof has the potential to make theorem provers more accessible to mathematicians and other researchers who are not experts in formal logic.
    *   **Improved Educational Tools:** The step-by-step verification approach could be integrated into educational tools to help students learn how to write rigorous mathematical proofs.
    *   **Advancement of Autoformalization Research:** The insights gained from this research can inform the development of more robust and efficient autoformalization techniques.

*   **Justification of Score:**

Given its innovation in the autoformalization approach, solid experimental validation, and potential for significant impact on the field, the paper demonstrates value. It's hindered somewhat by limitations in model size and dataset complexity, thus warrants a middle score.

**Score: 7**
